# kastellan — Development Roadmap

Sequenced feature list. Items are added whenever a feature decision is made
and ticked off as they ship. Order reflects expected build sequence — earlier
items unlock later ones.

> **How to update.** When we agree on a new feature, append it to the most
> appropriate phase (or a new phase) at the position that respects
> dependencies. When a feature ships, change `[ ]` → `[x]` and condense its
> entry to a **terse one line** with the commit/PR hash — enough to document
> the build sequence, no more. Pending `[ ]` items keep their full design
> context. **Pure refactors, test-module lifts, file splits, clippy/CI gates,
> flake fixes, and isolated bug fixes are NOT recorded here** unless they're
> load-bearing for remaining work — git history and the handover archives
> (`handovers/archive/`) are the durable record for those.

---

## Phase 0 — foundations (COMPLETE, archived)

Workspace skeleton, the Linux bwrap backend (+ the AppArmor `unprivileged_userns`
workaround), the macOS Seatbelt port, `kastellan-protocol` and the first sandboxed
worker, Postgres bring-up, the audit log and the LLM-router stub — all shipped
between `140eec5` and 2026-05-10, with no open items left.

**The "Phase 0 cont. — Service supervisor" section below is deliberately NOT
archived**, despite the Phase-0 heading: it is where the supervisor / installer /
operator-env track still accumulates work (#458, #504, #531, #552 all landed there
in 2026-08). The heading is historical, the content is live.

Per-item detail and commit hashes: [`archive/roadmap_phase0.md`](archive/roadmap_phase0.md).

## Phase 0 cont. — Service supervisor

- [x] Linux `systemd --user` unit generator + `systemctl --user` driver (`supervisor/src/systemd_user.rs`) — 2026-05-10
- [x] macOS LaunchAgent plist generator + `launchctl bootstrap` driver (`supervisor/src/launchd_agents.rs`) — 2026-05-08
  - [x] `run_launchctl` bounded by `bounded_command::run_capped` (20s) so a churn-degraded GUI domain can't hang a lifecycle call at 0 CPU — 2026-06-25 (residual read-only `print` calls: [#353](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/353))
- [x] Core daemon `ServiceSpec` (`specs::core_service_spec`) + cross-OS `default_probe()` + e2e against the real binary — 2026-05-09
- [x] `kastellan.target` that brings up Postgres + core — native systemd `.target` (Linux) / readiness-based bundle (macOS); inference is an external health-checked dependency, workers are core-owned (spawned on demand). `TargetSpec` + `Supervisor::{install,start,stop,uninstall}_target` + `specs::kastellan_target_spec()`; `ServiceSpec.after`/`part_of` ordering fields; gated `target_smoke` e2e ran live against `systemctl --user` — branch `feat/kastellan-target-bring-up`, 2026-06-06
- [x] Auto-restart with backoff on worker crash (Option K). `ServiceSpec.restart_backoff: Option<RestartBackoff { max_delay_sec, steps }>` (additive, `#[serde(default)]`, `None` = old constant-`RestartSec=5` behaviour). systemd backend emits `RestartSteps`/`RestartMaxDelaySec` (252+; older systemd warns-but-loads) inside the `keep_alive` block; macOS launchd warns-and-ignores at install (no equivalent knob — same posture as `after`/`part_of`). core+postgres specs wired with a 5s→300s/8-step curve. Builder test modules lifted to siblings to stay under cap — branch `feat/restart-backoff`, 2026-06-07
- [x] **One-command operator install** — `kastellan-cli install` / `uninstall [--purge]` takes a freshly-built tree to a running, supervised, per-user Kastellan (Postgres + daemon under `systemd --user` / launchd). Copies all binaries (atomic) into a flat `~/.local/lib/kastellan/` prefix + assets into `~/.local/share/kastellan/`, shells out to idempotent `kastellan-db-init --username $USER` (peer-auth match), writes a flag-generated `~/.config/kastellan/kastellan.env` (mode 0600) plus an operator-owned `kastellan.env.local` overlay beside it, both carried by `ServiceSpec.environment_files` → ordered systemd `EnvironmentFile=` directives (launchd folds them into plist `EnvironmentVariables` — no silent drop), defaults per-OS since [#568](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/568) / `2519e4eb` (2026-08-15) — Linux keeps Ollama `:11434` (`gemma4:26b-a4b-it-q8_0` + `embeddinggemma`), macOS uses oMLX `:8000` (`Qwen3.8-27B-8bit` + `embeddinggemma-300m-bf16`); memory-fit-checked for **every** backend, `ollama pull` only when local, enables linger (Linux), restart (stop→start) so reinstalls apply new artifacts, and verifies (PG socket + both services `active`, polled to 90s). Resolves HANDOVER open-question #6 (production install convention). `core/src/install/{plan,run}.rs` + `core/src/bin/kastellan-cli/install.rs`. PRs [#316](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/316) + review fixes — **DGX-verified post-merge (build + reinstall + `--no-start` + `secret list` all green)**, 2026-06-20
  - [x] **Operator config survives a reinstall — `kastellan.env.local`** — closes [#458](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/458) (PR [#528](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/528), branch `fix/458-operator-env-overlay`, **MERGED 2026-08-08 as `6e22a470` and DEPLOYED the same night**; gated on both hosts + live-accepted, then **re-confirmed post-merge in the steady state** — a plain `install` of `main` left `kastellan.env` stripped back to the bare model tag with none of the four keys, printed no warning and wrote no `.bak` because there was legitimately nothing left to destroy, and the running daemon's `/proc/<MainPID>/environ` still carried all five tuned settings plus `FORCE_ROUTING=1`). `install` regenerates `kastellan.env` from CLI flags every deploy, so hand-added keys were dropped and tuned values reverted **silently** — which on 2026-08-06 removed `KASTELLAN_MAIL_ENDPOINT` and cost the deployed bot its whole mail capability for two days: `mail.*` never registered, the planner fell back to `shell-exec` filesystem probing, and the only symptom was the bot answering *"I do not have permission to access your files"*. No code defect anywhere. **The design rests on a measurement**: `EnvironmentFile=` overrides `Environment=`, later files win, `-<missing>` starts fine — verified on the DGX with a transient unit, so one overlay listed second fixes lost keys *and* reverted values, and the regenerated file can also override the unit's own `KASTELLAN_EGRESS_FORCE_ROUTING=1` (hence part 3). `ServiceSpec.environment_file: Option<PathBuf>` → `environment_files: Vec<EnvFileRef{path, optional}>`, applied in order; `optional` is a real field because launchd's `read_to_string(...)?` hard-errors on the absence that is `kastellan.env.local`'s normal state. `parse_env_file`/`merge_env` lift out of the macOS-only `launchd_agents::builders` into a `cfg`-free `supervisor/src/env_file.rs` (#511's `atomic_write` precedent) — **and the DGX gate proved the point: predicted 3067, landed 3069, the +2 being those two tests running on Linux for the first time**, having never compiled on the DGX or in CI. Part 2: pure `diff_env_files` + `kastellan.env.bak` (written only on a non-empty diff — and, after the review below, never *overwriting* an earlier backup, since the second **destructive** install is the case that mattered and it would have replaced the only copy of the first one's losses) + a warning naming every dropped/changed key — **key names only, never values**, since the operator reads values from the backup. Part 3: a disabled force-routing now emits a `warn!` + `egress.force_routing_disabled` audit row instead of nothing (`main.rs` had no `else` arm at all), phrase as a `const` per #516/#524/#525. **Five of six review findings were defects in the PLAN TEXT**, transcribed faithfully and invisible to test counts and clippy — worst being `diff_env_files` comparing a repeated key's *first* value where systemd and our own `merge_env` take the *last*, so `old=A=1\nA=2` vs `new=A=1` (a real revert) reported **nothing at all**. The whole-branch review then found three cross-task defects per-task reviews structurally could not: `README.md` still taught the retracted behaviour, `upgrade_from_git.sh` could not read the overlay it now recommends, and `uninstall --purge` destroys overlay + backup while the daemon recommends `--purge`. **Live-accepted**: with the settings in *both* files, one install printed `dropped:` ×4 + `changed:` ×1, wrote the backup, left the overlay's mtime untouched, and `/proc/<MainPID>/environ` then carried all four keys plus the `-ctx64k` tag and `FORCE_ROUTING=1` — the exact 2026-08-06 sequence, survived. **Post-review `/fixall` wave (`44529b36` + `86e3d85a`)** — a six-reviewer pass (code, tests, silent-failure, type-design, comments, simplification) found two blockers. (1) **The fix had the disease it cured:** `preserve_and_report_env`'s `let Ok(old) = read_to_string(..) else { return Ok(()) }` called *every* error "first install", so `InvalidData`/`PermissionDenied` fell through to the caller's truncating `write_private` — one non-UTF-8 byte destroyed `kastellan.env` with no backup, no diff and no warning, in the one case where the operator has no other copy. Only `NotFound` is forgiven now. (2) **The whole-branch review fixed `README.md` and missed the manual:** `docs/user/manual/04-setup-and-run.md`, the "New here?" entry point, still taught "edit `kastellan.env` and restart" in five places including the two mail keys — and pointed `KASTELLAN_MATRIX_PEERS`, which has **no installer flag and is never emitted**, at the file that drops it every install; the search-broker cutover runbook did the same, with a stale force-routing step beside it. *Sweeping one file is not sweeping the class.* Also: **the parser diverged from systemd and a DGX measurement proved it** — 5 of 7 cases differed (both quote kinds stripped, value whitespace trimmed, `export FOO=`/`;FOO=` dropped rather than becoming keys named `export FOO`/`;FOO`), so one hand-written overlay produced two different runtime environments on the two first-class platforms; the measurement is now the test fixture. The launchd fold moved into the `cfg`-free `env_file::fold_env_files`, so **ordering and per-entry optionality — half a cross-platform guarantee — finally compile and test on Linux** (the #511 argument, third instance). Plus an absolute-path guard for `environment_files` (systemd drops a relative `EnvironmentFile=` and starts with *no* environment — fail-open, journal-only), the overlay path threaded from `Layout` rather than re-derived, a platform-correct apply hint (on launchd an overlay edit does nothing until the next `install`, which must carry the original flags), `upgrade_from_git.sh` taking a repeated key's **last** value (the shell twin of the `diff_env_files` defect an earlier round fixed), and `force_routing_disabled_message()` replacing a test that asserted a const contains a substring of *itself*. Filed not fixed: [#529](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/529) (unit/plist files at umask default while `kastellan.env` is 0600 — bounded by `~/Library` being `drwx------`), [#530](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/530) (a quoted `EnvironmentFile=` path is silently dropped by systemd — fail-open, needs a measurement first), [#531](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/531) (nothing confirms the overlay was found — "absent by choice" and "absent by typo" are the same silence), [#532](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/532) (`ServiceSpec` serde: a renamed field silently defaults; latent, never persisted today). Gate at `44529b36`: DGX **3085 / 0 / 53** (`TEST_EXIT=0`, +16 = supervisor +9, `install::` +6, notice +1 — fully accounted), clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0; Mac supervisor **96 / 0**, `core --lib install::` **48 / 0**, **native** clippy clean for supervisor *and* core (the branch now has a `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]` arm in `core`, so the Mac core lint is load-bearing). Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-08-08-operator-env-overlay*`.
  - [x] **…and the overlay is now observable, and its `EnvironmentFile=` directive is no longer droppable** — closes [#531](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/531) + [#529](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/529) + [#530](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/530) + [#532](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/532) (PR [#549](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/549), **MERGED 2026-08-13 as `15e8ba28` — Mac gate only; the Linux gate is still owed, see HANDOVER**). The four follow-ups #458's `/fixall` wave filed. **#530's measurement ruled out both fixes the issue proposed:** run against the DGX's live user manager rather than reasoned from the docs, systemd parses `EnvironmentFile=`'s whole rvalue as a literal path and then demands `path_is_absolute` — so a **bare path containing spaces is applied** while *every* quoted form (double, single, `-`-prefixed) is **dropped** with `path is not absolute, ignoring:` while `systemctl start` still returns 0. Quoting was the defect, not whitespace; the directive is now emitted bare. That removed an *accidental* safety property — quoting prevented newline injection by making the path invalid — so `validate_env_file_path`, shared by both backends at install, now rejects control characters as well as relative paths. **#531 is deliberately stronger than the issue asked**: a daemon cannot observe which file a variable came from (systemd merges before exec, launchd bakes into the plist at install), so instead of printing a key count it compares each declared key against the process's own environment — which also catches an overlay ordered *before* the generated file, where `kastellan.env` wins and the tuning is silently overridden, i.e. #458 itself. Absence is logged too, naming the path read, because #531's own scenario is an overlay created *after* install at a mistyped path. Key names and counts only, never values, pinned by two tests. **#529**: `create_staging` now opens 0600, so unit files and LaunchAgent plists stop landing at the umask default — which mattered most on macOS, where the backend folds the env files into the plist and a 0644 plist was a world-readable copy of the 0600 `kastellan.env`; `rename` preserves the inode, so a reinstall repairs a legacy file. **#532**: `deny_unknown_fields` on `ServiceSpec` + `EnvFileRef`. **A five-reviewer `/fixall` wave then found three defects in that first cut, all of the same family — the observability layer reporting a broken overlay as healthy.** `unapplied.is_empty()` is vacuously true over an empty set, so an overlay declaring ZERO keys (what you get by writing `export KEY=value`, which systemd's grammar has no notion of) was logged `all present in this process`; the parser discarded its own rejects, so one refused line in a six-key file read as a clean five; and the daemon re-read the file after `inspect_overlay` had already parsed it, opening a window where the count and the verdict described different contents plus an untested second error path. One `check_overlay` + `render_overlay_check` now does the whole daemon-side job — one read, one verdict, one wording, severity returned rather than logged so which outcomes deserve `warn!` is testable. The wave also removed a **contradiction on a published API**: `build_unit_file`'s doc still promised `EnvironmentFile=` was escaped through `quote_if_needed`, with the old rationale block left sitting directly above the loop that now emits it bare — false, on a `pub` re-exported renderer whose "pure builder" shape invites direct callers. And `validate_env_file_path` gained a non-UTF-8 rule: the renderer emits via `to_string_lossy`, so `U+FFFD` substitution yields a *different* path that systemd silently skips on the `-`-prefixed overlay — #530's shape, passed by the control-character rule because `U+FFFD` is not a control character. Gate at the pre-wave tip: DGX **3177 / 0 / 53**, `TEST_EXIT=0`, clippy `-D warnings` clean, **+15 reconciled exactly**. After the wave, Mac supervisor **111 / 0**, `core --lib` **1566 / 0 / 1**, clippy `--workspace --all-targets` exit 0, and cross-target clippy for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` clean — **but the DGX was unreachable, so the post-wave tip has no Linux gate and its two new `systemd_user` driver tests have never executed.** Five mutations run, all killed — including one on a test that initially passed *because* it recomputed the count inline instead of calling `inspect_overlay`, the "names a property it cannot observe" shape. **Live-accepted on the DGX**: unit files went **664 → 600** with all three services back `active` (so systemd genuinely reads owner-only units in production), directives render bare, channel up on attempt 1, and the daemon logs `operator overlay applied: …/kastellan.env.local (5 keys, all present in this process)`. Two platform legs each measured on the host that can see them — a 0600 systemd unit starts, a 0600 LaunchAgent bootstraps — and a reminder that `cargo test` on the Mac compiles **zero** `systemd_user` tests, so #530's rendering test was absent from the Mac suite entirely. Filed, not addressed: [#548](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/548) (PG e2e tests install units into the operator's real `~/.config/systemd/user/`), and from the `/fixall` wave [#550](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/550) (the *generated* `kastellan.env` still gets no end-to-end check, and on a no-overlay host its total loss renders as the reassuring normal-case line — the naive fix false-positives, since the overlay legitimately overrides its keys), [#551](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/551) (no path directive escapes systemd's `%` specifier), [#552](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/552) (`unquote` diverges from systemd for a value with content after a closing quote, e.g. `A="a b" # note`, which the new value comparison would report as a **false** `NOT fully applied` — probe recipe in the issue; unmeasured because the DGX was down).
  - [x] **…and the env-file parser now agrees with systemd where it continues past a closing quote, and on the whitespace class itself** — closes [#552](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/552) (branch `fix/552-env-file-quote-grammar`, PR [#556](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/556) open 2026-08-14). `unquote` stripped a quote only as a value's FIRST character and a matching one only as its LAST, so it diverged for any value continuing past a closing quote; since #549's `check_overlay` compares each declared value against the live environment, that renders a **false** `NOT fully applied` naming a key that DID apply — and on macOS it was never cosmetic, because `fold_env_files` bakes the answer into the plist. **The measurement overturned the issue's own predicted answer:** #552 expected `A="a b" # note` → `a b # note` and proposed "append the remainder", but systemd yields `a b# note` — the whitespace run immediately after a closing quote is dropped while the space in `A="a b"x y` → `a bx y` is kept, so that fix would have shipped a *third* wrong answer. *Third issue running whose stated remedy or root cause did not survive measurement (#530, #527, #552): an issue's remedy is a hypothesis.* The round-1 probe deliberately withheld one claim — which whitespace class the post-close skip uses — and **running that deferred probe found a second divergence**: every trim in the module was `char::is_whitespace` (Unicode) where systemd's is ASCII, so `<NBSP>Y=y` **invented an env var systemd never creates**, the `export FOO=bar` failure in new clothes. *A claim you decline to make is a lead, not a loose end.* Gate: DGX `cargo test --workspace` **3186 / 0 / 53** + clippy 0, Mac clippy 0, `kastellan-supervisor --lib` 113 Mac / 115 DGX. Four mutations across both rounds, all killed — the load-bearing one being "implement #552's proposed fix", which reproduces the issue's own predicted string.
  - [x] **…and an absent `data_ceiling` now resolves to the task floor instead of the most permissive class** — closes [#506](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/506) (branch `fix/506-data-ceiling-floor-resolved`, PR [#558](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/558) open 2026-08-14). `Plan::data_ceiling` defaulted to the constant `Secret`; the field is a **ceiling**, so the most *sensitive* class is the most *permissive* one — at rank 3 neither I1 (`ceiling >= floor`) nor I3 (`step.classification <= ceiling`) can fire, leaving a defaulted plan **not ceiling-constrained at all**. #505 shipped this described as fail-closed; it was the opposite. `Public` is not the fix either — it trips I1 on the `Personal` floor that `"my email"` infers, re-blocking the exact terminal plan #505 shipped to unblock. Resolution is **the floor itself**: the lowest ceiling that cannot spuriously trip I1 while leaving I3 real teeth. Serde cannot express it (a field default sees neither the floor nor sibling fields), so the absence survives deserialization as `None` and is resolved **once**, after `apply_floor_raise` (so an agent-raised floor is what gets resolved against) and before the audit write, L3 expansion and review. An unresolved ceiling is a **violation** (I0), never a second default — any constant there restores the fail-open in one direction or the spurious block in the other. `data_ceiling_source` joins the `plan.formulate` payload (27→28 keys), because a resolved ceiling reads exactly like a model decision and the old constant only ever announced itself in the daemon log. Four parameters grouped into `ClassificationProvenance` rather than suppressing `too_many_arguments` — one concept, always passed together, three same-shaped. *A mechanical `Option` refactor left one test compiling while still asserting the old default; all 62 auto-wrapped sites were then audited rather than trusted — the #539/#546 lesson in a new costume.* Gate: DGX **3211 / 0 / 53** + clippy 0, Mac `core --lib` 1588 / 0 / 1 + clippy 0; three mutations, all killed, incl. moving the resolution above the floor raise (fails with the pre-raise floor, `Some(Public)`). **Live-gated on the DGX 2026-08-14**: tasks re-run as 153/154, both **completed** (5 and 3 plans), `data_ceiling_source: declared` on all 8 plan rows, zero ceiling violations. The emission question answered by measurement — **0 of 320** historical `plan.formulate` rows carry a `Secret` ceiling and there has never been an omission warning, so the fail-open path was never taken on this host, which is exactly why both tasks were unaffected. *The live run therefore gated against regression but could not exercise the new `floor_resolved` branch — production never omits the field, so the PG e2e is the evidence and the live run only the safety net.*
  - [x] **The installer's worker list had silently lagged the workspace by five binaries** — closes [#504](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/504) (PR [#520](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/520), merged `41b21f36`). `optional_binaries()` was written when there were six workers; `mail` (#483), `email-in` (#496), `web-research` and both brokers were never added, and since the daemon discovers workers as `current_exe()`-relative siblings, **each was undeployable regardless of configuration** — one startup `ERROR` line, otherwise a completely healthy-looking install. Confirmed live on a fresh deploy of `main` (`"worker misconfigured; skipping","tool":"web-research"`), together with the worse half: the `cp` that worked around it for the mail worker during #492 left the deployed binary dated 2026-08-01 and no install has refreshed it since. The durable half of the fix is a drift guard in **`tests-common::installable`** — every binary **any workspace member builds** must be installed or in an explicit `NOT_INSTALLED` list *with a written reason*. Workspace-wide, not `workers/*`: a new `core`-level binary would go missing exactly the way `kastellan-worker-mail` did. It lives in `tests-common` because `linux-check.yml` is compile-only apart from `cargo test -p kastellan-tests-common`, making it the one guard that runs on **every PR**; a defect whose character is surviving checks that are not looking for it has to be caught the day a worker is added. The guard deliberately does not assert *which* list a binary lands in — only that the call was made consciously. Filed while writing the exemptions: [#519](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/519) — `kastellan-microvm-run` resolves from `$PATH`, not as an exe-relative sibling, so adding it to the list would copy it where the resolver never looks (recorded as an explicit exemption, not papered over). **Review round (`984dc53a`)** caught the guard having the same disease it was written to cure: it knew only two of Cargo's three binary-discovery modes, so a crate adding a bare `src/bin/*.rs` with no `[[bin]]` section would have passed green while the binary went uninstalled — dormant only because every such file in the tree happens to carry an explicit declaration. Discovery now covers `autobins` and dedupes candidates by claimed **path** before name (`prelude` renames its `src/bin` files, so a name-only check invents phantom targets), the hand-rolled line scanner was replaced with the `toml` parser already in the workspace (it read a commented-out `# "workers/retired",` as a live member — the #479 shape), and `optional_binaries()`'s docstring no longer claims a missing binary always costs "one `ERROR` line": the two broker sidecars log *nothing* when absent, and `search-broker` backs two tools rather than one. Mutation-checked: a bare `phantom_tool.rs` now fails the guard and passed before. DGX **3000 / 0 / 53**, clippy clean.
  - [x] **The mail tool now accepts the ids localmail actually emits, and `full_headers` works for the first time** — closes [#527](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/527) + [#500](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/500) (PR [#536](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/536), branch `fix/527-500-mail-id-contract`, **MERGED 2026-08-09 as `ddda13dc`**; the DGX had carried the branch build since its live acceptance and was put back on `main` 2026-08-10 — channel up on attempt 1, all five tuned settings + `FORCE_ROUTING=1` confirmed in `/proc/<MainPID>/environ`). `mail.get_message` was failing **14 of 26 live dispatches (54%)** while every other mail tool sat at zero. **#527's stated root cause was wrong and the measurement changed the fix:** localmail serialises every id as a JSON *string* (`"message_id":"37477"`), so the planner was faithfully echoing the previous tool call's output into a param demanding `i64` — a `ty: "integer"` annotation would have asked the model to contradict its own input, so the systemic `ToolParam.ty` idea was split out as [#534](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/534) to be judged on its own merits. The 14 failures split three ways: 7 numeric strings, 3 pasting the adjacent `next_cursor`, and 4 sending `{{message_id}}` for a template engine that does not exist — a habit appearing on this tool and **nowhere else in the audit log**, hence provoked by its own description rather than by the planner (a base-prompt change was declined with that evidence). `workers/mail/src/ids.rs`'s `LocalmailId` widens the accepted JSON *types* while still yielding a validated non-negative `i64`, so the URL-reaching value set is a strict **subset** of before (the old bare `i64` accepted negatives). Its sibling `explain()` gives the planner text it can act on — and had to be **reordered** to survive `STEP_ERR_DETAIL_MAX = 200`: the first cut front-loaded generic prose so every class-specific phrase was clipped, and the worker-side e2e asserted on the raw JSON-RPC message and was *true while the guarantee was false*, because the clamp is applied core-side downstream. #500: the service reads a query param named `headers` and derives the flag from its value, so `?full_headers=true` was dropped by FastAPI — measured live, `?headers=full` returns 19 headers and `?full_headers=true` none. **Why nothing caught any of it:** `mock_localmail` served numeric ids on the three routes the mail tool uses while serving correct string ids on the two `email-in` uses, and the `#[ignore]`d gate whose stated job is catching that drift **had drifted itself** (asserting `results` where the live list route returns `messages`, and reading ids with `as_i64()` so every row was skipped); it now passes against the real service for the first time. Gates: DGX **3108 / 0 / 53**, clippy clean; Mac targeted suites green. **Live-accepted**: the type-error class went 14 occurrences over 8 days → **0** since deploy, and the question that cost task 142 six plan iterations and 240 s completed as task 145 in **4 iterations / 81 s**. Filed not fixed: #534, [#533](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/533) (`shell.exec` refuses 40/81 because the planner is never shown the argv0 allowlist — same root class), [#535](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/535). Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-08-09-mail-id-contract*`. **Post-review `/fixall` wave (2026-08-10):** six reviewers confirmed the fix and found it **under-enforced rather than wrong**. The only test pinning #527 ran in **no CI job** — reverting `LocalmailId` → `i64` left 31 of 33 mail tests green, the one failure sitting in a target `linux-check.yml` never executed, so the fixture self-consistency tests ran every PR while the behavioural pin ran nowhere (`cargo test -p kastellan-worker-mail` added, hermetic, 0.58 s). `explain()`'s second cut quoted the rejected value FIRST, delivering `e.g. 374` — a truncated example reading like a wrong id — for any value ≥48 chars, which includes the live 64-char hex cursor; the test asserting the example fits probed with a 4-char stand-in. Fixed **structurally**: the value is quoted LAST in every arm, so the fit no longer depends on input length, and the worst case is derived from `HEAD_MAX` itself. The widening onto `account_ids`/`folder_ids` had carried `message_id`'s advice with it, telling the planner three times to repair a parameter that was never wrong (`IdField` now carries name, example and cursor-plausibility). #500 was verified against the live service **nowhere** — its evidence was a comment, and both tests pinning it asserted the worker sends a string the test itself wrote into a fake — so it gained a behavioural hermetic test plus both directions on the live gate; and `deny_unknown_fields` closed the same shape on the worker's own inbound side (`{"headers":"full"}` was accepted and silently produced a *compact* fetch). `STEP_ERR_DETAIL_MAX` moved to `kastellan-protocol`, deleting a hand-synced copy whose staleness would have been invisible on both sides. `scripts/mail/live-shape-gate.sh` **refuses** to run without the env rather than letting the `#[ignore]`+skip-as-pass double guard report a meaningless green, and the gate gained `/v1/search`'s string id, the `?headers=full` legs, and a per-leg status check so an expired token stops reading as schema drift. All four fixes mutation-verified. Filed not fixed: [#537](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/537) (`mail.search`'s `filters.*_ids` bypass the widening; needs a live measurement first), [#538](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/538) (`workers/mail` has no dev-deps, so its e2e hand-rolls a second localmail mock). Final gate at the merged tip `17f49cf7`: DGX **3116 / 0 / 53**, `TEST_EXIT=0`, clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0, exactly 4 `[SKIP]` (all `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_ENABLE`) — **+8 on the 3108 baseline, predicted before the run and exactly the wave's new tests**; Mac `worker-mail` **38 / 0** + e2e **3 / 0**, matching the DGX.
  - [x] **`mail.search` tells the planner which order it returned** — closes [#559](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/559) (PR [#562](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/562), branch `fix/559-mail-search-ordering-note`, gated **3232 / 0 / 53** on the DGX at `cfb75064` + clippy 0 on both hosts, **live-gated 2026-08-14** at the pre-amendment tip `08a6f24b`, which gated 3227). Relevance order is not date order, so *"most recent …"* questions got a best-match answer from any year; `sort: "date"` already existed and worked, so the fix is visibility, not capability. `sort` is now always sent (default `rank`), every response carries a self-describing `ordering_note`, and the parameter description states the consequence. **Load-bearing constraint, and the reason the design is what it is:** a successful step reaches the planner via `extract_scannable_text` — string values only, **keys discarded**, capped at 4 KiB — and `serde_json::Map` is a `BTreeMap`, so the note had to be a full sentence under a key sorting *before* `results` or it would have been silently clipped like #536's repair advice. Live: task 155 answered correctly in 3 plans vs 5, but the audit log shows plan 1 already passed `sort: "date"` — **the parameter description did that, not the note**, whose live evidence is still owed. Filed: [#560](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/560) (fabricated `message_id`, not fixed) and [#561](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/561) (paging a date-sorted search silently restarts in rank order — predates this fix; being fixed localmail-side). **#561 fed back into this PR:** `cfb75064` stopped defaulting `sort` when paging, because sending one contradicts the cursor's own ordering and would have *narrowed* the localmail fix to a single option. A change that is behaviour-neutral today can still constrain tomorrow's fix.

- [x] **GLiNER-Relex worker** (first `IdleTimeout` consumer) — Python package + Rust manifest/typed client; CPU/CUDA/MPS device resolution; Apple-`container` backend variant — slices 1–2.5 + macOS slice, PRs #88/#103/#118, 2026-05-18 → 2026-05-25
  - [x] **The venv's interpreter alias never binds into the jail** ([#650](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/650)) — **production, and it hit `browser-driver` too**, both through the shared `interpreter_deps::resolve_interpreter_root`. `uv` lays a managed CPython out as `cpython-3.13.14-linux-aarch64-gnu/` with a minor-version **symlink alias** `cpython-3.13-linux-aarch64-gnu` beside it, and the venv's `bin/python` — hence every console-script shebang — names the **alias**. The resolver canonicalized, so only the `.14` directory bound; the alias sat in an unbound parent, `.venv/bin/python` dangled inside bwrap, and `execve` returned **ENOENT for a file that is present and readable**, surfacing as the contentless `Protocol(EarlyExit)` ([#613](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/613)). Fixed by giving the one return value two named jobs: `InterpreterRoot::dep_walk_prefix` (canonical, for the `ldd`/`otool` comparison) and `InterpreterRoot::bind_paths` (canonical **plus** every alias). **The admission rule is non-widening** — an alias is bound only when it canonicalizes to the canonical prefix, i.e. the same tree under another name, so Homebrew's `/opt/hb/bin/python3.12` (prefix `/opt/hb`, a much larger tree) is refused and a prefix that does not canonicalize is refused too. That proof is taken at **resolve** time while the backend re-resolves each bind source at **spawn** time, which the review round qualified in the docs and filed as [#659](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/659) (a residual: repointing an alias needs the agent's own OS user, already the threat model's worst case). Two new pure modules (`interpreter_deps::named_path` — `normalize_lexically` + `symlink_chain`; `interpreter_deps::root`), +23 unit tests, and `interpreter_lib_dirs` now takes the whole `InterpreterRoot` so passing an alias to the dep walk is not expressible. 15 mutants tried and 15 killed — but the review found the inventory had stopped at `root.rs`: the two lines that *are* the fix (`fs_read.extend(root.bind_paths())`) had no test, so the pre-#650 `push(dep_walk_prefix())` was a surviving mutant. Both entry builders are now pinned. DGX-verified on the host it was filed from: `gliner_relex_e2e` **4/4 with zero `[SKIP]`** and a real 1.3 GB model load, `entity_extraction_e2e` **16/16** under `ENABLE=1` — all five previously-failing tests. Review follow-ups deferred to [#657](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/657) (the module emits no diagnostics at all) and [#658](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/658) (transposable same-typed probes). **#660's S6 guard crossed this refactor at merge:** `guard_interpreter_root` now takes and returns `Option<InterpreterRoot>`, re-homed in `interpreter_deps::root`, and checks every `bind_paths()` entry (canonical plus aliases), not the canonical prefix alone. PR [#656](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/656), 2026-09-02
  - [x] **The gliner-relex e2es cannot be forced to run** ([#653](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/653)) + **the fixture flag dialect** ([#654](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/654)) — five independent `[SKIP]` gates and no way to demand a real run, which is exactly how the `interpreter_root: None` fixture bug survived for months. `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_REQUIRE_E2E` now works on the Rust side too, turning **six** preconditions into a panic naming itself: the opt-in flag, the sandbox probe, the supervisor probe, the venv shim, the weights snapshot and the **Postgres bring-up** — the last is not in the issue's five, but without a cluster the test body never runs either, so leaving it skip-only would have left the knob green on the premise it exists to abolish (the shared `pg_bin_dir_or_skip` and `skip_if_no_supervisor` stay skip-only for their **~70** other callers — 234 call sites in 69 files, counted; an earlier "~30" was low by half — so the decision is made in each suite's own `bring_up_pg`). #654: the fixtures gated on a strict `!= Some("1")` while production reads the same variable through `env_flag_enabled`, so an operator's `ENABLE=true` was a silent skip; both halves now take the one #459 dialect, and the `resolve.rs` comments that taught the strict rule — the ones that propagated it into #651's Python — are corrected. Mechanically one new module, `kastellan-tests-common::gliner_e2e`, holding the whole host-mode cascade **plus** the `GlinerRelexEnv` the three suites built identically; that folds in the last triplicated `resolve_worker_script`, and these are the copies that drifted in **both** #284 and #650. Existing probes grew `*_or_reason` siblings that return a reason without rendering a verdict, so the caller decides skip-vs-fail; `skip_if_sandbox_unavailable` is `cfg`-free as a side effect. **`LOCKDOWN_SHIM_BIN` is declared outside the `cfg(linux)` arm on purpose** — a mutation naming the wrong binary SURVIVED while it lived inside, because macOS compiles that arm away. +12 tests in `tests-common` (which CI runs on every PR), 12 mutants tried and 12 killed, A/B-proved end to end. ⚠️ **The DGX gate caught a unit test faking a `[SKIP]` line** — the first sweep reported 5 where four were real, because the test pinning the skip arm called `report_unmet`, which prints. Every `[SKIP]` now renders through the pure `tests_common::skip::skip_line`; a `[SKIP]` count is evidence, so nothing may fabricate one. **A five-agent review round then found the knob had a hole and the tests could not see it, and both were fixed on the branch before merge — proved by execution, not argued.** The hole: `memory_entity_link_e2e.rs` opened all six tests with a skip-only `skip_if_no_supervisor()` *ahead* of everything require-aware, so its own require-aware supervisor check was unreachable — `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/nonexistent … REQUIRE_E2E=1` reported **`6 passed` having loaded no model**, the exact false green the knob abolishes, in the very suite that had drifted before. Fixed structurally: the check moved into `bring_up_pg` beside Postgres, and neither suite imports the skip-only helper any more, so the ordering cannot return. The blind spot: two mutants survived a fully green run — `require_action()` returning a constant (knob permanently inert) and deleting `report_unmet`'s `eprint!` (every `[SKIP]` gone, so the `grep -c` audit reports a *clean* run — worse than the original bug, because the workflow would then be actively lying). Cause: the mutant inventory had been drawn from the *tested* functions, not the *changed* ones; `require_action`, `gliner_host_env`, `venv_shim_or_reason`, `workspace_root` and the Skip arm had no test at all. Fixed by `report_unmet_to(.., &mut dyn Write)` (a test reads the emitted bytes back without adding a real `[SKIP]` to the count it protects) and a pure `first_unmet_precondition` taking four `FnOnce` probes — which is what finally lets a test prove the short-circuiting by `panic!()`-ing inside a probe that must not run. **Six of six mutants now die**; `use_container_backend: false` is pinned for the first time; +18 tests. Also corrected: the operator-facing `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_ENABLE != "1"` log line in `manifest.rs` — the last copy in this worker of the misinformation #654 is about. Deferred, with issues filed rather than scope-crept: [#664](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/664) (a filtered-out `cargo test` still exits 0 under the knob; the Python half guards this with `pytest_sessionfinish`, the Rust half has no equivalent and libtest offers no hook) and [#665](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/665) (`python_exec` + `browser_driver` carry the identical #654 dialect skew in five doc/log sites). PR [#663](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/663), 2026-09-03

- [ ] **`context_manager`**: token-budget + task-completion + wall-clock reset triggers. **Design borrowed from openworker's `coworker/compaction.py`** (auto-compaction spec, 2026-07-28), whose five load-bearing choices are all ones a naive implementation gets wrong: (1) the trigger is `min(threshold_pct × context_window, cap_tokens)` (0.8 / 250 k) — the **cap** is the non-obvious half, and exists so a 1 M-context model still compacts early, because quality and latency degrade well before the nominal limit; (2) the newest slice kept verbatim is a **token fraction of the trigger (0.25), not a turn count** — one huge tool loop must not starve the working set; (3) user messages are preserved **mechanically** (clipped, capped to the newest N), never left to the summarizer to remember, *and* the cap is load-bearing — without it the preserved block re-grows across repeated compactions until it reclaims the window it just freed; (4) tool results are the first casualty ("a file read 40 turns ago is better re-read") — Kastellan is strictly better placed here, since an oversized result is already a `{handoff_ref, summary_head}` placeholder (`core/src/handoff.rs`), so compaction drops the head and **keeps the ref**, leaving the body re-fetchable instead of destroyed; (5) **the persisted transcript is never modified — only the outbound view is.** That last one is not a preference for us: `audit_log` is the record of what the agent was told, and a compactor that rewrote history would break the property the whole audit surface exists to provide. The summarizer call itself runs tools-off with a modest ceiling (~3 k). **Two Kastellan-specific constraints their design has no analogue for:** the summary is LLM output re-entering the prompt at *higher* trust, so it must pass `escape_untrusted_body` at the same site the `<recalled>` / `<l1_insights>` bodies do (adversary #6 in the threat model), and the summarizer call is a `llm-router` dispatch like any other — it needs its own audit row and must respect the task's `data_ceiling`, or compaction becomes an unaudited egress of the whole conversation.
- [ ] **Tiered session digests — fill `MemoryLayer::L4`** ([#629](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/629)). **Design borrowed from Headlong's `design/tiered_memory.md`** (cross-project study, 2026-08-27; write-up in [`docs/devel/notes/2026-08-27-headlong-borrowings.md`](notes/2026-08-27-headlong-borrowings.md) §4). L4 is declared in `db/src/memories.rs`, accepted by the DB, and written by nothing; `prompt_assembly::assemble` has no episodic block at all, so cross-task continuity today is whatever L2 row someone happened to write. A logarithmic rollup pyramid over `audit_log` fixes it in bounded space: fanout `F=10`, tier *k* covers `F^k` rows, `⌈log_F N⌉` tiers, higher tiers rolled up **from the tier below** (not re-summarised from raw — that is what makes it exponentially cheap), **built forward-only from a start marker** so enabling it costs nothing up front. Three properties to keep: the raw log is the source of truth and the tiers are *an index, not testimony* (each rollup cites `audit_log.id` ranges so the agent can drill); the system must still work with every rollup deleted; and the new `<history>` block is model-authored, so it escapes via `escape_untrusted_body` like `<recalled>`, **not** verbatim like `<l0_meta_rules>`. Pairs with `context_manager` above (same paging problem: window = fast memory, log = backing store). Depends on [#628](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/628) for cheap citation. **Not** adopting Headlong's `unified_progressive_resolution_memory.md` (an HNSW-style redesign of the whole memory model, and unimplemented on their side too) — L4 digests are purely additive.
- [ ] **Reset snapshot writer** (compact context → memory before reset) — writes through the L1 promotion path (embed-lazily-after-dedup, degrade-and-warn), never a raw `insert_memory`, so a snapshot is recallable and inherits the L0 `PolicyViolation` guard.

- [x] **Large-tool-result handoff cache** — in-memory per-task content-addressed `HandoffCache` (`core/src/handoff.rs`). Oversized `Ok` results (serialized JSON > `DEFAULT_RESULT_BYTE_CAP` = 64 KiB) are stashed in the dispatcher layer (`ToolHostStepDispatcher::dispatch_step`, after `tool_host::dispatch` returns — the sealed chokepoint is untouched) and replaced with a `{handoff_ref, byte_len, summary_head}` placeholder. A reserved `handoff`/`fetch` built-in, intercepted before registry lookup (no worker spawn), returns clamped slices (`MAX_FETCH_BYTES` = 256 KiB). `task_id` threaded through `StepDispatcher`; entries purged at task terminal in the lane runner; per-task byte budget + global `MAX_TRACKED_TASKS` backstop bound memory. Blocked injection outputs are never stashed (they arrive as the tiny `injection_blocked` placeholder, under cap); operator `memory l3 run` path (`task_id <= 0`) passes through verbatim. In-memory (not the unwired `Workspace` scratch) per the design; `web-fetch`'s 100 KiB text cap is the realistic worst case. — branch `feat/handoff-cache`, 2026-06-08. Review follow-ups (2026-06-09): the stash branch now has real-worker dispatcher coverage (`scheduler_step_dispatch_e2e::dispatcher_stashes_oversized_ok_result_only_for_positive_task_id` — shell-exec echo > 64 KiB, asserts placeholder + cache round-trip + purge + the `task_id = 0` passthrough gate + the `handoff.stashed` audit row), closing [#198](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/198); the global backstop now `warn!`s when it evicts a bucket; the fetch intercept documents why it (unlike stash) is ungated on `task_id`. Planner-surface follow-up (2026-06-09, PR [#200](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/200)): `assemble_system_prompt` now emits an always-present, drift-proofed `<handoff>` block (`render_handoff_block()` in `core/src/prompt_assembly/assemble.rs`, interpolating the source-of-truth `HANDOFF_TOOL`/`HANDOFF_METHOD_FETCH` constants plus the byte caps `SUMMARY_HEAD_BYTES`/`MAX_FETCH_BYTES`, with a unit test cross-checking the placeholder fields, the real `fetch(...)` return shape, the fetch params, and both caps) teaching the planner the placeholder shape + the `fetch` step protocol — the mechanism is no longer inert. Deferred: per-tool `result_byte_cap` override (YAGNI); on-disk store.
- [x] **Registry-driven `<tools>` planner block** — the planner LLM is now told which tool workers exist. New `WorkerManifest::tool_doc() -> Option<ToolDoc>` (defaulted `None`); `ToolDoc{name,method,summary,params}` static compiled-in literals (trusted, unescaped). `assemble_registry` collects docs **only** in the `Resolution::Register` arm → a Disabled/Misconfigured worker is neither dispatchable nor advertised. All 7 workers implement `tool_doc()` (methods verified against live handlers); registry drift guard pins `doc.name == name()`. Threaded `build_tool_registry → Arc<[ToolDoc]> → PgSystemPromptBuilder::with_tool_docs → assemble_system_prompt`; rendered between `<recalled>` and `<handoff>`. `agent_planner.md` rewritten to point at `<tools>` and drop the old "there is no web-search tool / do not search the web" prohibitions (the sole reason the Matrix "I have no web search tool" refusal happened). Advertisement only — no dispatch/allowlist/reviewer change, no new dependency. Subagent-driven TDD (6 tasks); final opus review READY TO MERGE (0 Critical/Important). Mac-green (core lib 1154/0/1, workspace build exit 0, `clippy -p kastellan-core --all-targets -D warnings` clean). Operator still needs SearxNG + `KASTELLAN_WEB_SEARCH_ENDPOINT` + the allowlist row for a live web answer. — branch `feat/planner-tools-block`, PR [#437](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/437), 2026-07-11. Spec/plan `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-07-11-registry-driven-tools-block-planner-prompt*`.
  - [x] **…and the block now names each allowlisted tool's permitted VALUES, not just its parameter shapes** — closes [#533](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/533) (branch `fix/533-advertise-tool-allowlists`, PR open 2026-08-10). #437 told the planner which tools exist; it still never showed the operator allowlist those tools enforce, so the planner guessed membership one ~50 s plan iteration at a time. **The issue's own diagnosis was wrong and measuring changed the scope:** `tool_allowlists` was **empty until 2026-06-22 07:37** (`created_at`; the `tools.allowlist.add` action has existed since 2026-05-14, so the absence of earlier rows is evidence), so the "11 of 40 bare names for binaries that ARE allowlisted" claim compared historical dispatches against *today's* allowlist — all 15 bare names fall inside the empty window, and the absolute-path rule that same commit added to `agent_planner.md` gave **zero bare names in the 66 dispatches since**. The real defect was 25 of 66 (38%), every one a correctly-formed absolute path to a genuinely non-allowlisted binary. Fix: pure `render_allowed_values(kind, entries) -> String` (sorted so the output is a pure function of the *set* — `list_for_tool` is `ORDER BY argv0 ASC`, but that is Postgres *collation* order while this is byte order, and a pure renderer must not inherit determinism from one caller's query — escaped, each entry individually **backtick-quoted**, and capped at `ADVERTISED_ALLOWLIST_MAX = 30` with **both** numbers named when it cuts) plus `AdvertisedTool { doc, allowed }` — a wrapper rather than a new `ToolDoc` field, so `ToolDoc` keeps its "compiled-in ⇒ trusted, never escaped" invariant and `allowed` is **private**, constructible only through `with_allowlist`/`without_allowlist`, with the renderer module-private: nothing in the tree can build one around raw DB text. Decoration rides `registry_build`'s existing allowlist fetch, so the `WorkerManifest` trait is untouched and all **four** allowlist workers (`shell-exec`, `web-fetch`, `web-research`, `browser-driver`) are covered by one seam. **Empty is not absent:** a declared-but-empty allowlist advertises the consequence rather than the state — deliberately not "the allowlist is empty", which reads as *unrestricted* to a model. That consequence is **per kind**: argv0 says every call will be refused (true — `shell-exec` string-matches `argv[0]`), while domain says the tool can reach nothing and calls will fail or return no usable content, because `web.research` has no host parameter and still searches. Both name the **daemon restart**, since the allowlist is read once at bring-up. **The final review caught what would have made it inert:** the branch touched no file under `prompts/`, and `agent_planner.md` bound the planner to exactly three `<tools>` fields, so the new line had no authority; the scoped re-review then caught that fix over-generalising "pick one verbatim" onto the three `Domain` workers (`web.research` has no host parameter at all). Gate: DGX **3128 / 0 / 53** at `bc9c9a67`, clippy clean — predicted 3128 and landed exactly; Mac `core --lib` 1558/0/1. **Live-accepted with a clean A/B**, same question ~7 min apart: task 146 (old build) spent 5 iterations on 5 *different* non-allowlisted binaries, 5/5 refused, `task.failed`; task 147 (new build) used the allowlisted `/usr/bin/python3` every time, **0/5 refused, `task.completed`**. Filed, not addressed at the time (#539: `shell.exec` reports a signal-killed child as success — `/usr/bin/python3` returns `exit_code: null` with both streams empty; raised in priority because #533 makes the planner *pick* that binary; fixed by the entry below). **Second `/fixall` wave (2026-08-11)** — five reviewers over the whole branch found the escaping seam sound and the *truthfulness* axis under-defended, since the deliverable is an assertion in the system prompt. Fixed: the clause named `POLICY_DENIED` as THE allowlist-violation code, but browser-driver returns `RENDER_FAILED` (-32003 → `RPC_ERROR`, while its `INVALID_INPUT` is *also* -32001, so the two are inverted → [#543](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/543)) and web-research does not refuse at all; the empty-case claim was universal where the consequence is per-kind; **a single row `/usr/bin/ls, /usr/bin/cat` rendered as two permitted values, neither of them permitted** (entries are now individually backtick-quoted, and the prompt says the backticks are delimiters); rows the #459 screen had *already computed dead* were advertised as reachable — the inverted form of #533 — now withheld by a pure `endpoint_guard::partition_dead_rows`; the half-declared `allowlist_tool`-without-`kind` state now `warn!`s instead of silently un-advertising an enforced allowlist; and the cap now tells the *operator* what it withheld, not just the model. **The test that guarded the worst mutation ran a one-element loop** (its ctx registered only `shell-exec`, so the `false == false` direction it was named for never executed and "advertise every registered tool" survived); rebuilt on three fakes, which also gave Domain-kind its first advertised-surface coverage. Five comments were confirmed wrong, including a CHECK-constraint claim the branch's own HANDOVER already had right. Gate: DGX **3135 / 0 / 53** at the tip `9b21be85`, `TEST_EXIT=0`, clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, +7 reconciled to the seven new tests; Mac `core --lib` **1565 / 0 / 1** (also +7), clippy exit 0; **nine mutations, all killed**, each naming the test that caught it. Follow-ups filed: [#541](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/541) (advertised set ignores the row's `kind` column), [#542](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/542) (cap bounds count, not bytes), [#543](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/543), [#544](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/544) (`escape_untrusted_body` is C0-only — U+2028 survives), [#545](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/545) (collapse the two trait methods so the half-declared state is unrepresentable). Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-08-10-advertise-tool-allowlists*`.
  - [x] **…and a signal-killed child is now an ERROR, not a successful call with `"exit_code": null`** — closes [#539](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/539) (branch `fix/539-signal-death-is-loud`). Both workers that spawn children serialized `ExitStatus::code()`, which is `None` **exactly** on a signal death, into an `Ok` result — so a seccomp kill, a cgroup OOM kill and a CPU-budget kill were indistinguishable from a command that printed nothing, and the planner built correct-sounding answers on unreported failures. **Measuring widened the issue past its own title:** the kernel names the kill (`type=1326 … sig=31 syscall=198` — SIGSYS on `socket(2)`), and reproducing the jail's `--clearenv` shows the class is *every user- or group-name lookup*, because glibc NSS opens a socket for each and `WorkerStrict` denies it by design — `ls -l`, `id` and `whoami` die exactly as `python3` does. python3 itself is recoverable (`-S` works; **`-I` does not**, it never disables `site`), so the allowlist row stays. Fix: one pure `child_exit` module in the prelude (`classify` + a message budgeted against the shared `STEP_ERR_DETAIL_MAX`, diagnosis first and the caller's command last), `OPERATION_FAILED` in both workers, `ExecOutcome.exit_code: Option<i32>` → `end: ChildEnd` so the old line is a **compile error** rather than a silent regression, and a `[lib]` target for shell-exec, which was bin-only — no test in the tree could reach its handler, which is why this went unpinned for its whole life. **Five core e2e tests were resting on the bug**: `socket_attempt_is_contained_by_the_jail` asserted containment with `assert_ne!(exit_code, 0)`, which passed *because* `null != 0`. Their repair then reintroduced the same defect class — `contains("0 B out")` also matches `"10 B out"`, and both leak payloads are exactly 10 bytes — caught by the scoped re-review and anchored. Also documents the trap that `coreutils_smoke.rs`'s "a SIGSYS is a `BASE_ALLOW` gap" rule would set for the NSS case: following it would put `socket` in `BASE_ALLOW` and collapse the `Strict`-vs-`NetClient` boundary the seccomp allow-list exists to draw. (An earlier wording said it would "void `Net::Deny`" — **wrong mechanism**, corrected in the `/fixall` wave below: network denial is the private netns on Linux and Seatbelt on macOS, not seccomp, which is exactly why `WorkerMlClient` permits the whole socket family and stays `Net::Deny`.) **`/fixall` wave (2026-08-12)** — a five-reviewer pass found the branch reproducing its own defect class in the repair. All three `Ok` arms of the repaired containment tests still used `assert_ne!(exit_code, 0)`, which passes on `null`, so a reverted or stale worker binary took the `Ok` arm and went green **while reporting the exact bug under repair** — every new assertion lived in the `Err` arm, unreachable precisely when the fix is absent. Two net-denial tests had also silently lost their `assert!(out.get("exit_code").is_some(), "dispatch broken, not contained")` guard, leaving `unwrap_or_default()` to pass vacuously on any result without a `stdout` leaf — including the `injection_blocked` placeholder `dispatch_with_sink` substitutes for a blocked Strict-profile worker, and python-exec is on that list. Both fixed via `tests-common::signal_death` (`assert_nonzero_exit`, `stdout_of`), which also **closes [#547](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/547)**: the zero-output anchor was hand-copied at six sites and `assert_is_signal_death` at three, now one definition each, with the anchor delegating to `child_exit::reports_zero_stdout` — the crate that renders the string. Also: neither call site's `(stdout_len, stderr_len)` order was pinned (a swap passed the whole suite while three e2e files read the stdout count as a *containment* proof) → named-field `Captured`; the cross-platform test was **2/3 inert** — `SIGBUS` differs too (7 Linux, 10 macOS) and was the one divergent constant it omitted, while `SIGXCPU`/`SIGSEGV` agree on both hosts and so cannot distinguish `libc::X` from a literal; and the SIGSYS gloss told python-exec's caller to "try `python3 -S`", which `python_args()` **already applies unconditionally** — one `cause()` table served two workers with disjoint remediations, now split by a `Caller` enum. That split shortened the SIGSYS prefix 186 → 151 chars, which incidentally fixed a defect nothing had noticed: at 186 the M10 guarantee (`ran: /usr/bin/python3`, 202 chars) was **clipped by the 200-char `STEP_ERR_DETAIL_MAX`**, so it held only in the audit row. **The 15 tests ran in no CI job** — `linux-check.yml` tested two crates, none of them these three — now fixed, prelude `--lib`-scoped so its bwrap-dependent integration files cannot skip-as-pass. Gate: DGX **3162 / 0 / 53**, `TEST_EXIT=0`, clippy `-D warnings` `CLIPPY_EXIT=0`; **+12** (4 `child_exit`, 2 python-exec, 6 `tests-common::signal_death`), predicted and landed exactly. Three mutations run, all killed: transposing the byte counts, hard-coding `SIGBUS`, and reordering the message so `ran:` precedes the counts — the last being the mutation the *previous* round's clamp test could not catch. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-08-11-signal-killed-child-is-loud*`.
  - [x] **…and the advertisement is now bounded, kind-correct and unforgeable** — closes [#545](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/545) + [#541](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/541) + [#544](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/544) + [#542](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/542) (branch `fix/540-followups`, PR [#555](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/555) open 2026-08-13). The four small follow-ups #540's review wave filed, all in the seam it built. The *production* code is `cfg`-free — but that did **not** make a Mac gate sufficient, see the gate note at the end. **#544 widened under review:** the issue named U+0085/U+2028/U+2029, but `escape_untrusted_body`'s `< 0x20` rule also let through U+009B — the 8-bit CSI a terminal reads exactly as the ESC it *does* neutralise, and the daemon log is plaintext an operator reads in a terminal — so the class became `char::is_control()` (category `Cc`: C0 + DEL + C1, which subsumes NEL) plus U+2028/U+2029 plus the bidi controls; *taking the category removed the seam, enumerating exceptions would have kept it*. The docstring now also names what it does NOT buy (zero-width chars, homoglyphs). **#542** bounds the rendered list at 4 KiB over the **escaped, quoted, joined** text — escaping expands, so a raw-byte budget bounds a different string than the one reaching the prompt — withholding **whole** entries, because a clipped path advertised as permitted names a value that is not permitted and the planner would burn an iteration on a refusal the feature invented; an over-long entry is skipped rather than terminal, and "nothing fits" gets its own sentence rather than a `showing 0 of 1` with an empty list. One `select_advertised` is now the single definition of "advertised" for both the renderer and the operator warning, with `total()` a method — #549's numerator/denominator defect pre-empted rather than repeated. **#545** replaces two independently-defaulting trait methods with one `AllowlistDecl { tool, kind }`, deleting both `warn!` arms and the static guard test with them (a state the type system rejects needs no runtime detector) and replacing that test with one on what is still driftable — the `allowlist_kind_for_tool` scan the CLI validates against. **#541** carries the row's own `kind` through `ResolveCtx::allowlist` as `AllowlistRow`s and withholds a row whose kind disagrees with its tool's declaration: the kind picks the *wording*, so an argv0 row under `web-fetch` was announced as a reachable host. The column is **compared, not parsed** — an unrecognised kind must read as "not this tool's kind" rather than fail the whole registry build over one row — and the filter is **advertisement-only**, since narrowing the enforced set would silently remove capability from a correctly-configured deployed host ([#554](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/554) carries that half with its own gate). Pure-movement commit first: `registry_build.rs`'s 672 test lines lifted into a sibling module, dropping the production half back under the 500-line guideline. The `/fixall` round then found a fifth defect and the DGX gate a sixth. **An all-mismatched-kind allowlist is not an empty one:** when *every* row fails #541's filter the empty list fell through to the renderer's zero-rows branch, telling the planner "every call to this tool will be refused" about a tool whose calls succeed — enforcement is kind-blind — which is #533's failure re-invented from the opposite side and worse, since a planner told a tool is dead does not retry it. `partition_dead_rows` guards the same inversion for its own filter and structurally cannot guard this one (by then the mismatched rows are gone, so an all-mismatch looks like an empty table). Fixed by **adding a state** — `AdvertisedTool::with_opaque_allowlist`, which gives the count and says the values are still enforced while naming none of them — rather than softening the empty wording, which is true at zero rows and is what the three domain workers advertise on the live DGX today; suppressing the line was rejected because a tool with no `allowed:` line reads as *unrestricted*. The second review finding (the dead-row screen now judging only the declared kind's rows) was assessed and deliberately kept: a domain worker's net entries are built from its domain rows, so an argv0 row propping up the "some row is alive" count was never evidence about reachability — pinned with a test rather than left to be re-derived as a bug. **Gate: BOTH hosts, and the Mac one alone was not enough** — the DGX's first run executed *zero* tests, because #541's `ResolveCtx::allowlist` type change also reached two `ResolveCtx` fakes in `core/tests/` that are `cfg(target_os = "linux")` and so were never compiled on the Mac. Final: DGX `cargo test --workspace` **3203 / 0 / 53** + clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0; Mac `core --lib` **1582 / 0 / 1** (identical to the DGX, confirming the production code really is `cfg`-free) + clippy exit 0; `db --lib` **154 / 0**, and the one changed integration assertion (`postgres_e2e::tool_allowlists_round_trip_and_grant_shape`) **ran** against a real PG 18 rather than merely compiling. Eleven mutations across the two rounds, all killed. Findings no suite could see: a new function inserted directly above `advertisement_warnings` stole its doc comment, and a first #542 test probed a 40-char shared prefix so it matched a *withheld* entry inside a shown one. **Durable lesson: a type change's blast radius includes `cfg`-gated TEST files, so "the production diff is `cfg`-free" never licenses a single-host gate.**

Documents the degradation contract (lexical + `metadata @>` work; semantic + graph degrade gracefully — `semantic_search` already filters `WHERE embedding IS NOT NULL`). PR [#195](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/195) (`39a036a`), 2026-06-07. **Deferred follow-ups:** core-side caller wiring; per-namespace caps + oldest-eviction (openhuman quotes "max 50 KV entries, max 200 docs") — fits on `memories.metadata` as the namespace selector with no schema change, but does not block this surface; a graph-lane degradation test ([#196](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/196)).

Real `RouterEmbedder` threaded `spawn_scheduler`→`lane_loop`→`drain_lane`→`write_l1_promoted_row` for the agent-raised path; operator CLI `l1 add` stays NoOp. No `db` change. macOS live-PG 18: `memory_l1_promote_e2e` 12/0 (+3: embed-on-insert, lazy-on-dedup-skip, degrade-and-warn), clippy `-D warnings` clean. (Re)embeds every `layer = 1` row with `embedding IS NULL` — pre-#324 rows + operator-added `l1 add` rows (NoOpEmbedder by design) — through the **real** `RouterEmbedder`, so backfilled vectors match the forward path's on-insert ones. New db helpers reuse the existing dim/encode chokepoints: `load_unembedded_at_layer` (scan) + `set_embedding` (guarded `UPDATE … WHERE embedding IS NULL` → **idempotent + race-safe**). Core `reembed_l1_null(pool, &dyn Embedder) -> ReembedReport{scanned,embedded,skipped}` (new `core/src/memory/l1_reembed.rs`, degrade-and-warn per row, never fails the batch); CLI `memory l1 reembed` builds the embedder from `RouterConfig::from_env()`, prints `scanned=/embedded=/skipped=`. No separate audit row (each embed already logs `action='embed'`); no migration; **L1 only**. macOS live-PG 18: new `memory_l1_reembed_e2e` 3/0 + db/core units +4, clippy `-D warnings` clean. Branch `feat/325-l1-embedding-backfill`, 2026-06-21. **Review follow-ups (PR #327):** pure `reembed_batch_failed` predicate (`scanned>0 && embedded==0`) → aggregate WARN in `reembed_l1_null` + CLI **exits non-zero** on a wholly-failed batch (idempotent no-op still exits 0); +3 core units + a 4th mixed-batch e2e scenario (`memory_l1_reembed_e2e` now 4/0).

Core: shared `ReembedReport` lifted to `core/src/memory/reembed.rs`; `entity_reembed::{entity_embedding_text("kind: name"), reembed_entities_null}` (degrade-and-warn, mirrors `reembed_l1_null`). `RecallModes` gains `entity`; `ALL` + the new no-seeds default `SEMANTIC_LEXICAL_ENTITY` (used by `RecallParams::new`) enable it → runs on the cli_ask path; quarantine-filtered in production; RRF-fused.
