- [x] **Agent tool-loop recovery (PR [#337](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/337))** — fixes a live `plan_iteration_cap_exceeded` on a question the model knew the answer to. Three root causes (systematic debugging on the live DGX): (1) **blind replanning** — `plans_so_far_summary` collapsed failed steps to bare `"err"`, discarding `StepOutcome::Err { code, detail }`; new pure `render_step_outcome` surfaces `err: <CODE>: <detail>` (200-char clamp) to the planner + `agent_planner.md` guidance (answer directly from knowledge; only real tools exist; read the error, don't repeat a denied step; `shell-exec` argv[0] must be absolute) + `DEFAULT_MAX_PLANS_FAST` 3→5. (2) **LLM transport timeout** — the "`error sending request`" failure was the reqwest total `.timeout()` (30s) firing during a ~86s plan generation on the local 26B (a reqwest timeout displays identically to a send failure); `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` 30s→180s + `RouterError::Transport` now tags `[request timed out]`/`[connection failed]` via the pure `transport_kind_tag`. (3) **empty DGX allowlist** → seeded `/usr/bin/{cat,ls,python3}` (operator DB state). Verified live: distance question completes on plan 1; `/usr/bin/ls /tmp` runs `terminal_kind:ok` in the jail; a tool task formulates all 5 plans (no 30s cutoffs). TDD units (`render_step_outcome`, `transport_kind_tag`) + cap pins; clippy `-D warnings` clean on core/db/llm-router; deployed + restarted on the DGX. Branch `fix/agent-step-error-feedback`, 2026-06-22. **⚠ Open follow-up [#338](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/338):** ~~successful tool *output* is still fed back as just `"ok"`~~ — **DONE, next bullet.** **Review follow-ups (PR #337):** added a seam test pinning the worker-error → `map_dispatch_result` → `plans_so_far_summary` composition end-to-end; clarified the `STEP_ERR_DETAIL_MAX` clamp doc (rendered detail is ≤ cap+1 incl. the `…` marker); noted on #338 that the error `detail` (worker-supplied `rpc.message` for the RPC bucket) already shares the same injection surface, so the eventual guard must cover both halves.
- [x] **Forward entity embed-on-insert** — closes the deferred forward half of the entity-embedding arc (symmetric with #324 forward / #325 backfill, but for entities). `entity_extraction::batch_upsert::upsert_entities_and_relations` now takes a `&dyn Embedder` and embeds the entities it **just created** (`inserted == true`, the `xmax = 0` discriminator) through the shared `entity_embedding_text` chokepoint + guarded `set_entity_embedding`, so a freshly-extracted entity is searchable via the entity-similarity lane without a manual `entities reembed` run. Pure `select_new_entities(deduped, map)` selector (4 units); degrade-and-warn embed loop (an embed `None` / lost `IS NULL` race / write `Err` skips the row, never fails the upsert); embeds after the entity commit, before the relations phase. Conflict-hit (existing) entities are NOT re-embedded — still the backfill's job. `GlinerRelexExtractor` now owns an `Arc<dyn Embedder>` (`main.rs` shares the one `RouterEmbedder` across L1 + entities); `NoOpEntityExtractor` path unaffected. macOS PG18: new `entity_forward_embed_e2e` 3/0 (embed-on-insert + lane surfaces, conflict-not-reembedded, decline-leaves-NULL) + `entity_extraction_e2e` 16/0 + `entity_reembed_e2e` 4/0 + `memory_entity_link_e2e` 6/0 regressions; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean. No migration, pure-Rust → DGX not required. Branch `feat/entity-forward-embed-on-insert`, 2026-06-21. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-06-21-entity-forward-embed-on-insert*`.
- [x] **Feed successful tool output back to the planner ([#338](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/338))** — the success-half symmetric to PR #337's error-half, and the blocker for every tool-using task. `render_step_outcome` (`core/src/scheduler/inner_loop.rs`) collapsed a successful `StepOutcome::Ok(Value)` to the bare `"ok"`, so the planner never saw a step's output and re-issued the same successful step until `plan_iteration_cap_exceeded` (live: 5 identical `/usr/bin/ls /tmp` plans). **Key finding:** the injection-guard requirement was already met upstream — `tool_host::dispatch` screens every worker result over the first 64 KiB and `tool_dispatch::dispatch_step` stashes any `Ok(v)` >64 KiB to the handoff cache; since the caps are equal, every `Ok(v)` reaching the render is already screened + ≤64 KiB. **Fix (TDD):** the `Ok` arm renders a bounded head via `injection_guard::extract_scannable_text` (`STEP_OK_SUMMARY_MAX`=4 KiB) as `"ok: <head>"` (`…` on truncation), render stays screen-free by design; `prompts/agent_planner.md` updated so the planner answers from the output instead of re-running. **Security fix (final review):** the `fetch_handoff` branch returned its slice *unscreened*, and since `tool_host` only screened a stashed body's first 64 KiB, a fetch at `offset ≥ 64 KiB` could surface an unscreened tail (a hole *opened* by the render change) — closed with new `tool_dispatch/fetch_screen.rs::screen_fetched_data` (Strict/fail-closed), screening each served slice at the dispatch chokepoint → blocked `data` replaced by a withheld-note placeholder; the invariant now holds via both chokepoints. macOS: inner_loop 31/0 (+4), fetch_screen 3/0 (real Block exercised), `cli_ask_e2e` 7/0 (incl. plan-cap pin), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` CLEAN. Pure-Rust → DGX not required for the unit gate; live "ls /tmp count" acceptance is the remaining operator gate. Follow-ups [#339](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/339) (global summary budget) + [#340](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/340) (clearer blocked signal + split-slice limit). Branch `feat/338-feed-tool-output-to-planner`, 2026-06-22. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-06-22-feed-successful-tool-output-to-planner*`.
- [x] **Global budget for `plans_so_far_summary` ([#339](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/339))** — #338 follow-up hardening. #338 raised the per-step term ~2000× (bare `"ok"` → up to `STEP_OK_SUMMARY_MAX`=4 KiB), and `plans_so_far_summary` re-renders every plan's every step every iteration, so the accumulated total (`max_plans` × steps × 4 KiB, `max_plans` operator-overridable) was unbounded. **(1) Refactor:** the rendering helpers (constants + `sink_screen_blocks` + `render_step_outcome` + the per-plan mapping) lifted out of the over-cap `inner_loop.rs` (575→481) into a new pure `inner_loop/summary.rs` (286); `plans_so_far_summary` is now a thin delegate. **(2) Budget (TDD):** new `RenderedStep{text,elidable}` + pure `apply_summary_budget(&mut [Vec<RenderedStep>], budget) -> usize` elides the **oldest** successful-step output heads first (→ `OK_ELIDED_MARKER`) until total step-text bytes ≤ `PLANS_SUMMARY_BUDGET`=**32 KiB**; errors, decisions, the injection withheld-marker, and the most-recent plan's heads are preserved (what the planner answers from next, so no #338 loop regression). Guards: never elide non-elidable steps, never *grow* a tiny `"ok: 9"` (`len > marker` check), idempotent. Budget bounds the dominant (step-output) term, not the small per-step framing. macOS lib gate: summary 17/0 (+8 budget units) + inner_loop 43/0 (no regression), `cargo clippy -p kastellan-core --lib -- -D warnings` clean. Pure-Rust, no migration, no OS-gated code → DGX not required. Final whole-branch review (opus): **READY TO MERGE**, no Critical/Important. Branch `feat/339-plans-summary-global-budget`, 2026-06-23. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-06-23-plans-summary-global-budget*`.
- [x] **Clearer injection-blocked signal to the planner ([#340](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/340))** — final #338-arc follow-up, **completing the planner-feedback arc (#337→#338→#343→#339→#340)**. When `tool_host::dispatch` blocks a worker result on the output injection screen, the placeholder `{ injection_blocked, score, reason_codes }` rendered through `extract_scannable_text` (string leaves only) so the planner saw just the reason-code string (e.g. `"ok: instruction_override"`) — an unintelligible gap that could tempt a re-run, unlike the `fetch_screen` placeholder which already carries a human-readable `note`. **Fix (TDD, pure-fn):** new `core/src/tool_host/injection_placeholder.rs` (72 LOC) — `WITHHELD_NOTE` const = `"[tool output withheld: failed injection screen]"` + `injection_blocked_placeholder(score, &reason_codes) -> Value` (adds the `note` string leaf; keeps the structured fields for audit-shape parity with `fetch_screen`); `dispatch`'s Block arm calls it. `prompts/agent_planner.md` gained a "withheld step — don't re-run it" bullet; `docs/threat-model.md` gained a subsection on planner-bound-output screening (the two `tool_host`+`fetch_screen` chokepoints) **and the known split-slice limitation** (a payload split across a 64 KiB boundary / two `fetch_handoff` slices can evade single-slice screening — inherent to streaming bounded-memory screening; sandbox + egress proxy remain the real boundary). macOS: `injection_placeholder` units 3/0, `tool_host` lib 43/0, `injection_guard_e2e` 6/0 against real PG18 + Seatbelt (placeholder-shape test now also pins the `note`), clippy `--lib --tests -D warnings` clean. Pure-Rust, no migration → DGX not required. Branch `feat/340-injection-blocked-note`, 2026-06-23.
- [x] **Planner reliability on a local reasoning model — the agent could not answer ANY question about the user's email** (branch `fix/planner-disable-thinking`, PR [#505](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/505) **MERGED 2026-08-02** as `6b083553`). Driven from a real Matrix message; **four independent faults, only one in the layer everyone suspected.** (1) **Host**: an `apt upgrade` installed a new kernel but left the NVIDIA module metapackage on the old one ⇒ no `nvidia.ko` ⇒ Ollama silently ran the 26B model **100% on CPU** (`"size_vram": 0`); the `linux-modules-nvidia-**fs**` package *did* upgrade, so the tree looked current. Operator-fixed. (2) **Deployment**: `supervisor/src/systemd_user.rs` never runs `systemctl --user enable`, so **kastellan did not survive a reboot on Linux** while macOS did (`launchd_agents.rs` sets `RunAtLoad=true`) — a cross-platform-parity break; DGX hand-`enable`d at the time, **code fix shipped 2026-08-03** (PR [#509](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/509), closes [#508](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/508)): `install` enables `<name>.service` and `install_target` enables `<name>.target` — the boot entry point, whose `Wants=` pulls the members in, so members are deliberately not enabled separately — with `uninstall_target` disabling symmetrically so no dangling `default.target.wants/` link is left behind. The `Supervisor` trait now states "install implies auto-start" as a cross-platform contract both backends must honour (launchd declaratively via `RunAtLoad`, systemd imperatively via `enable`), and the installer's `loginctl enable-linger` call is documented as the other half of the same guarantee — lingering starts the user *manager*, enabling gives it something to start. **Retrospective review of #509 (PR [#511](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/511), merged before review):** no bug in the fix, five follow-ups — `write_atomic`'s destination-derived staging path was the *actual* cause of the tmp-file collision #509 patched at the test-name layer (now a per-writer `tmp_path_for()` with `create_new` and cleanup after a failed publish, in **one** `cfg`-free `supervisor/src/atomic_write.rs` shared by both backends, so its seven tests run on both hosts instead of splitting 4-on-Linux/3-on-macOS — and the same destination-derived pattern in `install::run`'s `copy_exec`/`symlink_replace`, which left the "two concurrent installs still race" claim true one layer up, fixed with it); **DGX re-gate 2965 / 0 / 53, +13 = exactly the tests added**; a `builder.rs` comment still claimed "we don't enable by default"; `--no-start` skips linger so it arms units a headless host still can't start, which only a code comment said; the contract's "after a reboot" was false on both platforms for a host nobody logs into (launchd loads LaunchAgents at *login*); and a SIGKILLed smoke test can now strand an enable symlink the manager fails on every login. Filed not fixed: [#510](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/510) — **CI never runs #508's regression guard** (`linux-check.yml` is compile-only, both tests would `[SKIP]` on `ubuntu-latest` regardless, and there is no macOS job at all, so launchd code is never even *compiled* on a PR) — plus, from #511's own review round, [#512](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/512) (`kastellan-cli install` prints systemd-only guidance and health-checks via `systemctl` on macOS — **FIXED 2026-08-15** in [#568](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/568)'s review round, via `Supervisor::status` exactly as the issue prescribed, plus per-platform message consts and fail-fast on a supervisor *error* rather than 90 s of polling a query that cannot succeed) and [#513](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/513) (per-file atomicity is not per-*install* atomicity: overlapping installs can publish a mix). (3) **`llm-router`**: the local reasoning model emitted **15 094 chars of `reasoning` for 1 519 chars of plan** (222 s; inside the daemon a single call consumed a full **600 s** budget and still timed out, so raising `KASTELLAN_LLM_TIMEOUT_MS` is *not* a fix). Added `ChatRequest.chat_template_kwargs` + `RouterConfig::disable_thinking` (default ON, `KASTELLAN_LLM_DISABLE_THINKING=0` opts out) emitting `{"enable_thinking": false}` — the OpenAI-compat extension Ollama and vLLM both honour — applied in `dispatch_local` as a property of the local leg; caller value wins; unset ⇒ field not serialised ⇒ byte-identical payload. Measured **222 s → 51 s**. (4) **`plan_parser` — the fault that hid the rest**: `parse_plan_lenient` re-emitted the *strict*-path error whenever the lenient path failed, and the strict path always describes the markdown fence, so a well-formed fenced plan that merely omitted a required field was reported as `expected value at line 1 column 1` ("your output wasn't JSON") when the real error was `missing field 'steps'`. **Cost the entire session** until the raw completion was logged. Now the lenient error is returned whenever the input contained a `{`. Also: `AgentError::Decode` captured the raw completion but its `Display` renders only `detail`, so it reached nobody — the structural facts (`detail`, `raw_len`, `has_brace`, `finish_reason`, token counts) are now `warn!`-logged, with the raw head at `debug!` because a planner completion restates the user's mail verbatim and the daemon log has none of `audit_log`'s access control (`docs/threat-model.md` gained a "User data in the daemon log" subsection). And `Plan::data_ceiling` `#[serde(default)]`s to `Secret` (local planners routinely omit it on terminal plans) with a warn on every use, while **`steps` is deliberately NOT defaulted** (an empty `steps` marks a terminal plan, so defaulting it would turn a *truncated* plan into a silently-terminal one); `agent_planner.md` hardened. **Review correction (2026-08-02):** that default was originally shipped and documented as *fail-closed*; it is **fail-open**. `data_ceiling` is a **ceiling**, so the most sensitive class is the most *permissive* value — at rank 3 both I1 (`ceiling >= floor`) and I3 (`step.classification <= ceiling`) pass vacuously, and a plan omitting the field is not ceiling-constrained at all. Docs, warn text, test names and the planner prompt corrected to say so; the behavioural fix (resolve an absent ceiling against the task's `classification_floor`, plus audit provenance) is [#506](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/506) (correction shipped in PR [#507](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/507), **MERGED 2026-08-03** as `d37df573`) — `Public` is *not* the fix, since `"my email"` infers a `Personal` floor and a `Public` ceiling would re-block the very task this branch unblocked. **#507's own second review round (2026-08-03) narrowed the severity claim further:** it is not "I1 and I3 vacuous, I2 still enforced" for every plan. L3 expansion stamps each expanded step's `classification` with `plan.data_ceiling` and runs *before* the reviewer, so a plan that both omits the field and carries `invoke_skill` has **all three** invariants pass vacuously. Still confined to that review stage — nothing outside `cassandra/deterministic.rs` reads `step.classification`. DGX re-gate at `c5ba7637`: **2950 / 0 / 53**, clippy clean, 4 `[SKIP]` (GLiNER tier only), +1 = the single test added. **Live: first end-to-end answers from the real 37k-message archive** — task 122 `completed` 386 s/6 iterations, task 124 `completed` **77 s/2 iterations**. **Gates: DGX 2949/0/53** (`--nocapture`, exactly 4 `[SKIP]`, all the env-gated GLiNER tier) **+ Mac 2831/0/23**, clippy `-D warnings` clean on both, **both +11** = exactly the tests added ⇒ nothing platform-gated. Known residual: the planner passes `mail.get_message`'s `message_id` as a **string** where the worker requires `i64`, burning an iteration in nearly every run.

## Phase 2 — Channels (read-only)

> **Primary channel decided 2026-06-12 (operator brainstorm):** **Matrix, self-hosted,
> single-user, federation OFF** (E2E via `matrix-rust-sdk` + `vodozemac`, vendor-neutral, zero
> marginal cost, all platforms via Element). **Email is the cross-transport fallback** (separate
> failure domain), used for low-trust async notifications, never commands *(superseded 2026-07-28 for inbound: the shipped slice-1 channel does ingest commands, gated by DMARC + per-pairing token — see the email entry below)*. Signal (`presage`
> fragility + ban risk) and Telegram (no bot E2E, centralized) rejected as primary. Homeserver
> runs as a supervised **conduwuit** unit; hosting is operator-selectable, fail-down: Tier A
> dedicated VPS (preferred) → Tier B existing WireGuard VPS (co-host = shared blast radius with
> network ingress) → Tier C the kastellan host itself ("poor man's" default). Matrix has **no
> single-user homeserver failover** — redundancy is the cross-transport email fallback, not a
> second homeserver. Full design + co-hosting security analysis + slice decomposition:
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-12-primary-communication-channel-design.md`.

- [x] **Channel-bus abstraction (build first)** — `core/src/channel`: `Channel` trait (inbound
  `IncomingMessage` stream + outbound `send`, dyn-safe) + pure security core — fail-closed
  `PeerAuthorizer`/`StaticPairings` (`auth.rs`), `classify_inbound` (authorize→`injection_guard`
  screen under `GuardProfile::Strict`→`tasks` payload, `ingest.rs`), `reply_for_completed_task`
  (finalized task→user reply, `route.rs`) — plus the `ChannelBus` runtime (`bus.rs`) over four
  seams (`Channel`/`PeerAuthorizer`/`ChannelEvents`/`CompletedTasks`; real `PgChannelEvents` +
  `PgCompletedTasks` over the `tasks` queue + `tasks_completed` NOTIFY, no new IPC). Channel tasks
  mirror the `ask` payload so the scheduler is unchanged; unpaired peers + injection are dropped +
  audited (hash only). 18 unit tests + hermetic `FakeChannel` full-loop e2e + PG-gated real-queue
  e2e; clippy `-D warnings` clean. No live transport / no `main.rs` wiring (slice #2). Branch
  `claude/zen-bell-6bn2ze`, 2026-06-12. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-12-channel-bus-abstraction.md`.
- [x] **Matrix inbound** (`MatrixChannel`, `matrix-rust-sdk`, E2E) — net allowlist scoped to the

  (`core/src/channel/matrix.rs`) + `ChannelBus::spawn` over `DbPeerAuthorizer`/`DbPairingService`/`PgChannelEvents`/

  `scripts/build-release.sh`; `db` pool `DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS` 4→16 (listener-slot starvation). Self-healing worker
  respawn (`MatrixChannel::supervised`, capped backoff, shutdown-aware); allowlist scoped to the homeserver's actual
  host:port (non-443 servers reachable). **Follow-ups (none blocking):** ~~inbound messages lost while the worker is
  down/respawning~~ **FIXED 2026-06-24 ([#321](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/321)):** on restart the worker reads

  default flipped to `=1`; new `matrix_client` seccomp profile (`net_client`+`ftruncate`) + a prelude **TSYNC fix**
  (`apply_filter_all_threads`) — the filter was previously a no-op binding only the main thread, leaving the `tokio` pool

  found by deploying the new death-report observability — was the **initial** worker being spawned via `spawn_blocking`, whose
  pool thread tokio reaps after ~10s, tripping bwrap's `--die-with-parent` PDEATHSIG → SIGKILL (death log: `signal: 9 (SIGKILL)`,
  no OOM/rlimit/seccomp). Fix: `MatrixChannel::supervised_self_spawn` does the initial spawn on the persistent driver thread too,

- [~] **Email inbound channel (fallback)** — **SLICE 1 MERGED 2026-07-31 (`bf8e850b`, PR #496); slices 2–3 open.** **DESIGNED 2026-07-28**, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-email-fallback-channel-design.md`, slice-1 plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-28-email-fallback-channel-slice1.md`. **Supersedes the original "IMAP inbound worker" framing:** localmail now exists and already ingests the mail over IMAP, so the channel polls its `/v1` subscription instead of speaking IMAP itself — no IMAP crate to sandbox, no mail credentials in a kastellan jail, and it reuses the force-routed egress path (private netns + 1:1 sidecar) that #491/#492 built out. **The extra-CA half of that path did NOT apply here until PR [#503](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/503)** — the channel's sidecar was a *transparent tunnel* with no MITM leg, so `KASTELLAN_EGRESS_UPSTREAM_EXTRA_CA` governed only the separate `kastellan-worker-mail` TOOL. On that branch the channel intercepts unconditionally and the env var applies to it too — but note the map is **global and host-keyed**, so one entry reaches every worker resolving to that address. The 2026-06-12 security posture is kept in full (DMARC pass + per-pairing in-body token), with the gate as **pure fns in core** so every rejection still lands in `audit_log`. Two single-origin workers (inbound poller, SMTP sender) is **forced by #492**: one worker holding both self-signed localmail and a public SMTP host is `MixedAllowlist`, which fails the spawn. **Pairing is operator-only** (`pair issue-token`) — the right posture for a spoofable transport. **Correction (review, spec D8):** the original reasoning here — "the token lives on the pairing row, so an unpaired sender can never present a valid one, hence the carve-out is unreachable over email" — was **WRONG and is refuted by the code**. An unpaired sender resolves to `Ok(None)` → `Rejected`, which *does* reach the carve-out, and `try_pair` would then mint a NULL-token row, permanently disabling DMARC+token for that address. Operator-only pairing is therefore enforced by an **explicit guard**, not by construction: `bus::handle_inbound` consults the carve-out only when `msg.evidence.is_none()` (i.e. only for a transport that authenticates its own peers, e.g. Matrix), and `DbPeerAuthorizer` refuses a token-less pairing row whenever the transport *did* supply evidence. Both guards are load-bearing — do not delete them as redundant. **SLICE 1 MERGED to `main` as `bf8e850b` (PR [#496](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/496)), 2026-07-31** (code-complete 2026-07-29 on branch `feat/email-fallback-channel-inbound`; merged content code-identical to the reviewed `658924ae`): sandboxed `email-in` worker, pure DMARC+token gate + evidence enforcement at the bus authorization chokepoint, core-side `EmailChannel` over the channel-generic `PolledWorkerDriver`, hermetic e2e (`core/tests/email_channel_e2e.rs`, 8 cases incl. the header-order-bypass and skipped-id-cursor-wedge fixes), and daemon wiring (`core/src/main/email_boot.rs`, mirrors `matrix_boot.rs`): a real `EmailEgress` (force-routed 1:1 sidecar when `KASTELLAN_EGRESS_FORCE_ROUTING` is on; the same legacy `--share-net` path Matrix takes when it's off — same rule, same invariant, no divergence) and a real `AckOnlyAudit` closure writing `channel.skipped_ack_only` rows for every silently-discarded message id. A partial config or worker-spawn failure **disables the email channel with a loud `error!` and leaves the daemon running** (`spawn_email_channel -> Option<ChannelBus>`, no `Result`, so no `?` can reinstate an abort) — the fallback channel must never be able to take Matrix, the scheduler, or the graceful-shutdown path down with it; design §6 says the daemon refuses to start *the email channel*, not the daemon. Corrected from an earlier abort-on-`?` posture by the final whole-branch review; see `email_boot.rs`'s module docs. Operator env block + traps documented in `core/src/install/plan.rs::render_email_help`. **KNOWN GAP found in review — CLOSED by PR [#503](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/503) (2026-08-01; DGX gate GREEN at 2938/0/53, clippy clean):** the force-routed sidecar was a transparent tunnel with no MITM leg, so a self-signed localmail was unreachable by this channel, force-routed or not. #503 gives `spawn_net_transport` a caller-chosen `Mitm` posture, has the channel intercept unconditionally, and selects the anchor once at boot. **Two limits survive:** the anchor map is GLOBAL and host-keyed (one entry reaches every worker on that address — the DGX shares `10.0.0.3` between localmail `:8443` and SearxNG `:8888`), and interception is the *precondition* for the #3b leak scanner, **not** coverage — nothing on this path is scanned ([#501](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/501)). **Post-PR review round (2026-07-30) — 9 findings, all fixed, DGX-reverified.** Two more High-severity defects in the same families as the pre-PR ones: (a) the gate was **still bypassable** via a legal RFC 8601 `method-version` — `method = Keyword [ [CFWS] "/" [CFWS] method-version ]` (§2.2) means `dmarc/1=fail` is a legal *real* verdict, which the parser read as method `dmarc/1` and therefore did not count, so the more-than-one-dmarc rule never fired and a forged unversioned `dmarc=pass` decided (confirmed a live ADMIT against the real code on the DGX before fixing); (b) a **401/403 on `message_detail` destroyed the message**, because `is_permanent` called it permanent ⇒ acked ⇒ monotonic cursor past it, though both are retryable after an operator action. Three Medium: `email.poll` was a 250ms poll loop, not a long poll (~4 req/s ⇒ ~345k requests, TLS handshakes and `egress.allowed` audit rows per day on an *idle* inbox — `POLL_INTERVAL` now 5s, ratio test-pinned); `channel.replied` asserted deliveries that never happened, so a new **`channel.reply_undelivered`** action is written when the transport refuses (in slice 1 `EmailChannel::send` fails unconditionally, so every email reply is routed-but-undelivered until slice 2); and the per-family-bus `senders` miss logged a misleading `warn!("…dropping")` on every delivered reply (now `debug!`; bus unification filed as [#497](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/497)). Four Low, incl. a removed comment now substituting a SPACE — deleting one *welded* tokens, so `mx(x)example.net` collapsed to `mxexample.net` and matched that configured authserv-id. **Also newly documented, and not fixable at this layer:** a non-escaping MX can be made to swallow its own verdict, leaving a forged pass as the only methodspec — which is precisely why the per-pairing token, not the DMARC verdict, is what makes the gate hold. See HANDOVER for the full list. **Slice 1 is DONE and on `main` — nothing outstanding at this tier.** The authoritative DGX-native gate at `658924ae` was GREEN: **DGX aarch64 2926/0/53**, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, only 4 `[SKIP]` (the one env-gated GLiNER tier); Mac 2808/0/23. The localmail dependency (Task 1) **merged 2026-07-30** as [hherb/localmail#223](https://github.com/hherb/localmail/pull/223) `0b6c5e05`. **Deployment is a separate gate:** the DGX's running `localmail-serve` predates that merge commit by three days, so it still serves the pre-cursor API and must be restarted before a live slice-1 deployment; the known MITM/self-signed gap above also applies to a live tier. Slices: **2** SMTP outbound + full round trip (`lettre`); **3** DGX deploy + live tier — the MITM + extra-CA half of slice 3 landed early in PR [#503](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/503), so slice 3 is now the `localmail-serve` restart, the deploy, and the live run.

Static contact allowlists rejected (forgeable) — `DbPeerAuthorizer` gates the bus on active `(channel,peer)` rows; the pairing carve-out is compare-only + operator-gated + single-use (`claim_code` atomic). **WebAuthn deferred** (no browser/CLI client surface yet). Daemon wiring (swap `StaticPairings`→`DbPeerAuthorizer` + pass `DbPairingService`) rides slice #2 Phase D. Branch `claude/zen-bell-6bn2ze`, 2026-06-12. Spec/plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-06-12-channel-pairing*`. (Pattern: ZeroClaw `security/{pairing,otp}.rs`.) (Slice #3.)
- [x] **Channel bring-up is supervised, not one-shot** — closes [#514](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/514) (PR [#516](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/516), **MERGED 2026-08-04** as `a21f8276`). Both boot modules tried exactly once and returned `None` on any failure, so a blip in the daemon's first seconds disabled the channel for the life of the process — bot deaf, every unit `active`, nothing further in the log. Live on the DGX 2026-08-03 (**12 h**; the trigger was a restart-window race, not the homeserver — the user manager was itself shutting down, so `systemd-run --scope` could not create the sidecar cgroup). New `channel::boot_supervisor::ChannelSupervisor`: unbounded retries over the existing pure `RestartBackoff`, driven by a `BootOutcome{NotConfigured|Started|Retry|Fatal}` taxonomy whose `Fatal` arm keeps statically-dead configs (`forced_localhost_homeserver` per #459, a partial `EmailConfig`) from becoming retry loops; pure `DowntimeEscalator` for the escalating "deaf for N minutes" line; `channel.started`/`channel.boot_failed` audit rows. Also closes [#502](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/502) (`SidecarHandle` gains the `Drop` its comments already claimed) — without it the retry loop would leak one egress proxy per attempt. Issue item 3 (`After=network-online.target`) **declined**: a `systemd --user` manager has no such unit (verified on the DGX). **Review round 2026-08-04:** the findings were all one class — *renaming a log line silently breaks everything that greps for it*. `scripts/upgrade_from_git.sh`'s post-deploy channel check had gone blind (both grep alternatives renamed away), `install::plan::render_email_help` still told operators to grep for `EMAIL CHANNEL DISABLED` while the supervisor emits `CHANNEL DISABLED` (its test pinned a *literal*, so it stayed green through the drift — both sides now interpolate `boot_supervisor::CHANNEL_DISABLED_LOG_PHRASE`), and `egress::persistent_net`'s comment still claimed the leak #502 had just fixed. The deploy verifier now polls (bring-up retries, so one snapshot is not a verdict) and is scoped to the current start by a pre-restart byte offset, which also kills its pre-existing stale-`✅` false green. Plus `shutdown_before_the_first_poll_starts_no_attempt` covering the loop-top shutdown guard. Gated both hosts at `e0a663df`: **DGX 2985 / 0 / 53** (`--nocapture`, 4 `[SKIP]` all the `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_ENABLE` tier) and **Mac 2864 / 0 / 23**, clippy `-D warnings` exit 0 on both. **A host reboot reproduced #514 live on the deployed pre-fix build** (2026-08-04 00:02 UTC): the matrix worker's initial sync failed `tunnel error: unsuccessful` before the network was up, the channel never started, and the bot was deaf until a manual restart that succeeded first try — that string is one of the branch's own scripted `Retry` cases, so the classification matches what production actually emits. Filed not fixed: [#517](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/517) (supervision covers *bring-up* only — a post-boot `ChannelBus` pump death is still permanent deafness with the supervisor parked, so the "silent bot ⇒ fatal config" reading holds for the boot window only) and [#518](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/518) (`channel.boot_failed` is written per attempt with no escalator gate, ~1440 rows/day/channel during a sustained outage). Spec + plan: `docs/superpowers/{specs/2026-08-04-channel-boot-supervision-design.md,plans/2026-08-04-channel-boot-supervision.md}`.

SPKI-SHA256 pin set (RFC 7469) overlaid on the proxy's upstream webpki validation (`workers/egress-proxy/src/pins.rs`: `spki_sha256`/`PinSet`/`chain_has_pin`/`PinningVerifier` + `build_upstream_client_config`), so a compromised public CA can't silently MITM kastellan's own high-value frontier/LLM egress. No new deps, OS-agnostic, byte-identical when unset. **Decoupled from the Phase-5 frontier path** (frontier LLM egress doesn't exist: `Router` denies all frontier calls + runs in-core via reqwest, not a sidecar). `cargo test --workspace` green + clippy `-D warnings` clean. **Still deferred to the first frontier worker:** routing frontier LLM egress through a pinned sidecar (needs the whole Phase-5 escalation path — Router-behind-a-sidecar + a real PolicyGate + frontier key from `db::secrets`); the pin plumbing is then ready to serve it. **Tracked in [#304](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/304):** a real-sandbox pin-enforcement e2e (pin-mismatch host → blocked `tls_pin`). The MITM re-origination leg can now trust an operator-provided extra CA in addition to webpki: `build_upstream_client_config(pins, extra_ca_path)` (pure `add_extra_ca_pem`) ← `KASTELLAN_EGRESS_PROXY_UPSTREAM_EXTRA_CA` (startup WARN) ← `proxy_policy`/`spawn_sidecar` (env + jail `fs_read`) ← `NetWorkerSpawn.upstream_extra_ca`. **Off ⇒ byte-identical; fail-closed** (bad PEM aborts startup); host-provisioned only; prod `force_route.rs` passes `None`. Proves out a force-routed self-signed localmail round-trip (see the mail-worker line), with a negative control asserting both `is_err` **and** a `mitm_failed: …` decision so the seam is provably load-bearing. **Two operator constraints, documented on `build_upstream_client_config`:** the cert must be a real CA that signed the origin leaf **or** a self-signed leaf with `basicConstraints CA:FALSE` — a `CA:TRUE` self-signed leaf is rejected at handshake time (rustls `CaUsedAsEndEntity`) even though `openssl verify` accepts it, and `openssl req -x509` commonly produces exactly that shape; and the anchor is trusted for **every host its sidecar can reach**, so it suits a single-origin worker only. **Deployed-origin operator config SHIPPED — [#492](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/492), branch `feat/492-upstream-extra-ca-wiring`, 2026-07-27.** `KASTELLAN_EGRESS_UPSTREAM_EXTRA_CA={"<private-ip-literal>":"/abs/ca.pem"}` → new pure `core/src/egress/upstream_ca.rs` (sibling of `cert_pins.rs`) → per-worker selection in `spawn_worker_maybe_forced` → `NetWorkerSpawn.upstream_extra_ca`. The review's ask was taken: the trust-scope constraint is **enforced, not documented** — an anchor is handed out only for a worker whose allowlist resolves to a **single private origin written as an IP literal** (`kastellan-net-classify::is_denied_range`, the proxy's own SSRF predicate, so the two cannot drift), and a refusal **fails the spawn**. The IP-literal rule is doubly motivated: trust widening is only defensible for an origin the operator controls, and the proxy's SSRF guard denies hostnames resolving into private ranges anyway (only allowlisted literals get the carve-out), so a name-keyed private origin would be unreachable regardless. Every configured PEM is **read at daemon startup** (fail-closed on unreadable/certificate-less) with a trust-widening WARN, so a typo aborts the daemon instead of surfacing on the first force-routed dispatch; `kastellan.env` carries both operator traps, pinned by a test that the block stays entirely commented. Unset ⇒ byte-identical. The `CA:TRUE` trap is real (it is why the DGX localmail cert was regenerated 2026-07-26) but is **not outstanding** — that cert is verified `CA:FALSE`, and localmail's source now emits non-CA leaves for fresh deployments. Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-upstream-extra-ca-operator-config-design.md`. Signature-hygiene follow-up: [#494](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/494) (carries a `Mitm::Transparent | Mitm::Intercept { upstream_extra_ca }` enum suggestion, which would make the posture/anchor contradiction unrepresentable). **Sidecar bring-up hardening shipped with it (`a4d9af56`):** the proxy now builds the upstream TLS config **before** binding the UDS + exporting `ca.pem` — those two files are the readiness signal, so validating after them meant a fail-closed abort published "ready" first and `spawn_sidecar` returned a healthy handle for a dying proxy (**latent for the slice-#4 pin path too**, not just the extra CA). `spawn_sidecar` additionally drains the sidecar's stderr (previously piped and unread, so every fail-closed reason and the trust-widening WARN went nowhere), catches an early exit with `try_wait` instead of waiting out `READY_TIMEOUT`, rejects an `upstream_extra_ca` that is relative or paired with `disable_mitm`, and logs the trust widening host-side.

  - [x] **…and supervised once it is up, not only while it starts** — closes [#517](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/517) (PR [#521](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/521), branch `fix/517-supervise-channel-liveness`). #516's `Started` arm parked on the shutdown oneshot and never re-examined the channel, but every `ChannelBus` pump has a terminal exit nothing watched — the completed-task pump returning when its listener gives up (replies stop going out), a per-channel task breaking on a closed `recv` (inbound stops coming in), or a panic in either — each reproducing #514's signature after boot instead of during it, and none writing a row. **The premise correction that shaped the fix:** `PgListener::recv()` already auto-reconnects in sqlx 0.9, so the `Err` arm is a *failed reconnect* (a sustained Postgres outage), not a dropped connection — the issue's smaller alternative ("make `next_completed` reconnect") would have fixed nothing sqlx does not already do, and could not cover a panic. New `channel::pump_liveness::{DeathBell,PumpLife}`: each pump holds a guard whose **`Drop`** rings, which is the only form that also covers a panicking or aborted task, and `notify_one` (not `notify_waiters`) so a death preceding the waiter is not lost. `StartedChannel` gains a liveness future (`new()` without one keeps park-until-shutdown exactly, so existing callers are unchanged); the loop selects shutdown-vs-death and re-enters itself, **stopping the channel on both paths** — a channel that lost one pump still holds the worker through its surviving per-channel task, whose drop is the RAII teardown, so abandoning it would leak what #502 fixed. Two guards keep the cure from being worse: a **flap guard** (`DowntimeEscalator::ran_long_enough` + `record_success`, 60 s = the backoff cap) so only a channel that stayed up resets the failure counter — without it a channel dying on arrival restarts at full speed forever, spawning a sandboxed worker per iteration — and a new `BootAudit::Died` → `channel.died` carrying `ran_ms`, kept distinct from `boot_failed` because "never came up" and "was up for six hours, then died" are different events. Both boot modules now open LISTEN/NOTIFY **before** spawning the worker: the dominant restart trigger is the Postgres outage that fails exactly that step, so the old order would spawn a worker and sit through a login on every retry of it. `boot_supervisor.rs` hit the 500-line cap → types lifted to `boot_supervisor/types.rs` (371 + 143). Mutation-checked: reversing the `biased` arms fails the ordering test and nothing else — and that test's first version passed either way, because `wait_or_shutdown` already declines the restart; what the bias actually prevents is a `channel.died` row written for a channel that was merely being shut down, and the test now asserts that instead. DGX **3025 / 0 / 53** (`--nocapture`, 4 `[SKIP]` all the `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_ENABLE` tier), clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0; Mac `core --lib` 1496/0 + `--bins` 87/0 + clippy clean. **Live-verified on the DGX** by stopping `kastellan-postgres` under the branch daemon: `outbound pump stopped` → `channel stopped working after running {ran_ms: 30557, retry_in_ms: 1000}` → retries failing on `PgCompletedTasks::connect` → `channel bus running {attempts: 6}` two seconds after Postgres returned, with `matrix worker logged in` appearing exactly twice in the run (so the five failed retries spawned no worker — the ordering fix). It also confirmed the audit limitation now documented in `pg_sink`: the outage that kills the pump also blocks the sink, so `channel.died` and the intervening `boot_failed` rows are absent and the daemon log is the record. **Review round 2026-08-05: one real defect, in the half nothing could observe.** `escalate_if_due` ran on *both* death arms, so a stable death did `record_success()` and then immediately `record_failure()` — opening an outage dated at the death that **nothing ever closed**, because the escalator learns about health only when a *stable* channel dies. A channel that came straight back and worked for hours still carried that instant, so its next flap reported every healthy hour as downtime, in the one line whose text asserts nothing sent to the channel has been received for that long — and past the 300 s threshold on the very first event, firing at once instead of after five minutes of real silence. The next outage is now opened by the first restart attempt that actually **fails**, the only version that stays correct when the restart *succeeds*: a real outage is dated one backoff delay late (≤60 s) where the eager version's error was unbounded. It survived the first round because escalation is a log line and nothing else, so the sequence had no test seam; the pure half is now `note_outage` over `Outage{Continues,Ends}` — the only place either half of the escalator's outage state is driven from — and two of its three tests fail before the fix (`Some(14400s)` where `None` is required). Two doc defects with it: `render_email_help`'s documented `audit_log` query never learned about `channel.died`, hiding the event this branch adds (the three action names are now substituted from `channel::actions`, and the test **iterates the set** so a new one is a failing test — #516's drift finding in its other direction, *adding* rather than renaming), and `record_success`'s docs claimed the supervisor calls it "on every successful start", which it does not and must not. Re-gated on the DGX at `3285a5e1`: **3028 / 0 / 53** (`TEST_EXIT=0`, +3 = exactly the new tests), clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0. Filed not fixed: [#522](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/522) — `STABLE_UPTIME` (60 s) sits below the escalation threshold (300 s), so a channel cycling "up 61 s → dead" is stable every time, resets the backoff and the escalator on every death, and never escalates at all; `RespawnRateAlarm` is built inside each `PersistentWorker`, so a channel restart discards its window and a death-rate alarm has to live in the supervisor. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-05-channel-liveness-supervision-design.md`.
  - [x] **…and what it says about itself is rate-limited, with an alarm for a channel that keeps dying** — closes [#518](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/518) and [#522](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/522) (PR [#525](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/525), branch `fix/518-522-channel-event-reporting-gates`, **MERGED 2026-08-06 as `4e215481`**; deployed 2026-08-08 as part of `6e22a470`). #518: the loud line was escalation-gated and the durable row was not, so a sustained outage wrote ~1440 identical `channel.boot_failed` rows/day/channel. #522: `STABLE_UPTIME` (60 s) sits below the escalation threshold (300 s), so a channel cycling "up 61 s → dead" was *stable* on every death — each one reset the failure counter **and** called `record_success()`, clearing the escalator — so the loud line never fired at any point while each cycle cost a sandboxed worker, its sidecar and a full login. One predicate answers both: `should_record(alarm_latched, alarm_spoke_now) = !latched || spoke`, driven by the alarm owning each stream (`DowntimeEscalator` for `boot_failed`, a new supervisor-owned `RespawnRateAlarm` for `died`). **This beat #518's own sketch** (`failures <= FIRST_N || escalated`) by needing no new constant — N falls out of the escalation threshold and backoff schedule — and by making the row and the line the *same* decision rather than two that agree today. New `boot_supervisor/reporting.rs` owns `ReportingPolicy` (both alarms, `Verdict{record, still_down, flapping}` with the two alarms as separate `Option`s so no precedence rule is invented) and replaces the `DowntimeEscalator` parameter of `ChannelSupervisor::spawn`; `RespawnRateAlarm` gains additive `with_repeat`/`in_storm`; `tests.rs` (719) split into `tests/{mod,bringup,liveness,reporting}.rs` as pure movement in its own commit. Row counts: 24 h bring-up outage 1440 → **~57**; 24 h flap ~2832 → **~1470**; a transient blip unchanged, because the gate only engages once an alarm has spoken. **Five things worth carrying.** (1) **Both latch reads happen AFTER the recording call** — reading `in_storm()` before `record()` reads better and is wrong, because `record()` is what re-arms on a cleared storm, so the first death of a *fresh* storm would be silently suppressed; mutation-pinned by `the_first_death_of_a_fresh_storm_is_recorded`. (2) **`channel.started` was gated and that was a design error caught mid-branch**: the latch only clears on a *later* death, so the start ENDING a storm was suppressed too and a channel that recovered for good never wrote another row — `channel.died` stayed the last durable event for a healthy channel. Now ungated; the extra ~1416 rows/day in a flap are the price of the answer to "is it up?", and the unbounded-forever case (a permanently-`Retry` bring-up outage) is fixed regardless. (3) **#522's own premise about `PersistentWorker` is wrong**, and so was this branch's first draft: `persistent.rs:167` builds the alarm before the job loop and `:220` swaps the *transport*, so it DOES accumulate across worker respawns — what it cannot see is a **channel** restart, which tears the whole thing down. Conclusion unchanged, attribution corrected; same lesson #521 learned about `PgListener`. (4) **#516's finding recurred inside the change that documents itself as immune to it** — `CHANNEL FLAPPING` shipped as a bare literal in `render_email_help` beside four const-interpolated neighbours, because the spec correctly predicted "no new *action* name, so the guard needs no change" and this was a *log phrase*: a guard protects the category it enumerates and nothing adjacent. Caught by the final whole-branch review. (5) `report()` now runs **before** the gated `emit().await`, so the alarm no longer sits behind a Postgres write whose reachable failure cause is Postgres going away. DGX **3042 / 0 / 53** at `22f8829d` (`TEST_EXIT=0`, `--nocapture`, 4 `[SKIP]` all the `KASTELLAN_GLINER_RELEX_ENABLE` tier), clippy `--workspace --all-targets -D warnings` exit 0; Mac `core --lib` 1513/0 + `--bins` 16+87 + clippy clean. **The +14 landed exactly on the prediction (3028 + 14)** and cross-checks against the raw diff (31 added `#[test]` attributes minus the 17 that merely moved in the split). Filed not fixed: [#523](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/523) (in the flap band a stable death clears the escalator latch, so a failed restart attempt writes an ungated `boot_failed` row every cycle — the naive fix is a bare stale-latch read *and* would suppress `cause`, the only forensic field) and [#524](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/524) (`CHANNEL STILL DOWN` is still a bare literal, third instance of #516's class). **Post-review `/fixall` wave (2026-08-06, `97488a34`):** the whole-PR review found no code defects but three gaps — the ungated-`channel.started` decision had no pinning test (new `a_start_during_a_latched_storm_is_still_recorded`, mutation run: gating the emit fails it and nothing else), `in_storm` narrowed `pub`→`pub(crate)` (the bare-latch-read footgun stays off the published API), and the plan doc got an as-shipped errata banner for the reversed `Started` gating (the spec had been corrected; the plan had not). Semver checked and dissolved: `ChannelSupervisor` postdates v0.2.0, so #525 touches nothing published. Gate: DGX **3043 / 0 / 53** at `97488a34` (`TEST_EXIT=0`, clippy exit 0, 4 `[SKIP]` same tier — landed on the +1 prediction); Mac `core --lib` 1514/0 + core clippy clean.   - [x] **…and the flap band's `boot_failed` rows are rate-gated too, with the last operator phrase const-lifted** — closes [#523](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/523) + [#524](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/524) (PR [#526](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/526), branch `fix/523-524-boot-failed-rate-gate`, **MERGED 2026-08-06 as `5cbab01e`**; deployed 2026-08-08 with #525 as part of `6e22a470`). #523: each stable death clears the escalator (`Outage::Ends` → `record_success`), so a transiently-failing restart attempt was evaluated against a freshly-cleared latch — one ungated row per cycle, unbounded, in exactly #522's regime. Fix: a second, deliberately **silent** `RespawnRateAlarm` on the failed-attempt stream inside `ReportingPolicy` (same `FLAP_ALARM_*` constants), fed by `note_failed_attempt` itself so the latch read follows a `record()` on the same alarm **by construction** — the issue's bare-stale-latch-read trap is structurally unrepresentable — with its voice deferred while the escalator has escalated, so a sustained outage keeps #518's row schedule instead of double rows per repeat. Row arithmetic: 24 h outage ~57 → **~53**; flap-with-failing-restarts **unbounded → first 5 + one `cause`-bearing row per 30 min**; plain flap unchanged. Three mutation checks, each failing exactly its named test. #524: `CHANNEL_STILL_DOWN_LOG_PHRASE` interpolated into the `error!` line and the help block — the third and last #516-class literal there; every operator phrase in that block is now const-driven. SLICE 1 (rootfs) MERGED as `8dbff298` (PR [#470](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/470)); SLICE 2 (the VM entry) MERGED as `ae1a9318` (PR [#472](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/472)); SLICE 3 (live render + budget measurement) MERGED as `61890c48` (PR [#474](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/474)), post-review hardening in-PR (`9ae425d3`, spec §8.5).**
  - **Slice 3 — the live render, 2026-07-19.** **A real page now renders inside the micro-VM through a real egress sidecar — the first time in this repo, in VM *or* host mode.** Slices 1+2 stopped at the `CONNECT` line (their stub proxy 503s, so the render deliberately failed), leaving end-to-end TLS, the response body, Playwright's post-JS extraction and the reply text untested. **A hermetic origin is impossible here:** browser-driver's sidecar runs no-MITM transparent-tunnel (`disable_mitm_for` names it), so Chromium does its own end-to-end TLS and must trust the ORIGIN's certificate — a self-signed loopback origin would need a CA in Chromium's NSS store (the deferred MITM-of-browser work), and weakening `DEFAULT_LAUNCH_ARGS` with `--ignore-certificate-errors-*` was rejected outright (production must not be loosened to make a test pass). This is exactly why the host-mode `forced_render_of_loopback_page_through_sidecar` navigates `https://` at a plain-HTTP server and settles for the decision row. **So slice 3 uses a real public HTTPS origin**, and both tiers drive the PRODUCTION `SingleUseLifecycle::with_force_routing(...).acquire(...)` manager rather than a hand-wired `NetWorkerSpawn` — strictly stronger, because the manager derives `disable_mitm` by calling `disable_mitm_for` **itself**, so the property is observed rather than restated. **Both slice-2 budgets are now MEASURED, not reasoned, and both confirmed:** `example.org` renders in **1.61 s = 1.8%** of the 90 s `wall_clock_ms`; a heavy Wikipedia article (38 408 B of text) peaks at **628 MiB VMM RSS = 30.7%** of the 2048 MiB `mem_mb`, closing slice-1 spec §10.4's worry that the `/tmp` tmpfs holding Chromium's shm could OOM the guest. Neither budget changed (trimming the wall clock was considered and rejected: it must cover a cold page cache, a slower origin and a heavier page). Memory is measured by sampling the Firecracker VMM's host RSS — guest RAM is faulted in lazily, so it tracks what the guest actually touched, tmpfs included — and **a missing sample fails loudly rather than skipping the assertion**. Two corrections found by running it: the egress action is `egress.allowed` not `allowed`, and a `tls_intercepted == false` assertion was added. **Negative-case verified:** tightening the memory threshold 85%→20% makes the test fail loudly with the intended message and the same reproducible reading. **No production code changed.** Debt lodged and since RESOLVED: [#473](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/issues/473) (`real_web_fetch_through_sidecar` was a hollow `#[ignore]`d scaffold that passed trivially) — implemented 2026-07-20 on the same real-public-origin pattern, but with `tls_intercepted: **true**` as its load-bearing assertion, since web-fetch is MITM'd where browser-driver is transparently tunnelled. The hermetic origin the issue suggested does NOT work: under MITM the *proxy* validates the origin and trusts `webpki_roots` only. **DGX: full workspace 2590/0/50 + `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, both exit 0, 4 `[SKIP]` (the known gliner-relex opt-ins only) — exactly +2 ignored over the 2590/0/48 slice-2 baseline.** Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-browser-driver-microvm-live-render-design.md`.
  - **Slice 2 — the VM entry, 2026-07-19.** `KASTELLAN_BROWSER_DRIVER_USE_MICROVM=1` (atop the `ENABLE` gate) registers browser-driver as a Firecracker VM worker. `browser_driver_firecracker_entry` mirrors `web_fetch_firecracker_entry` with five load-bearing differences: empty `fs_read` (a VM shares no host paths in; no NIC ⇒ no local DNS), **no `lockdown_shim`/`LANDLOCK_RW`** (host-mode mechanisms — the shim seccomp+Landlocks a bwrap-spawned Python venv worker (#281) and is not staged in the rootfs, so requiring it would be a boot failure; in VM mode the boundary IS the VM), `mem_mb: 2048` vs 1024 (Firecracker enforces it as total guest RAM and it must cover Chromium **plus** the `/tmp` tmpfs `--disable-dev-shm-usage` redirects shm into), the in-rootfs `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`, and `wall_clock_ms: 90_000` (cold VM boot + Node driver + Chromium cold start). The `resolve()` branch short-circuits the **entire** host-side resolution rather than just binary discovery as in web-fetch — host mode resolves a venv/interpreter/lib-dirs then fail-closes `Misconfigured` on the missing lockdown shim, none of which exists on a VM-only deployment — and reads **both** flags via the unified `flag_enabled` dialect so `USE_MICROVM` is never an implicit opt-in. **The main win is test-side:** slice-1 spec §10.4 flagged the guest path as "an instruction, not a mechanism" (three unlinked copies), so the e2e's hand-rolled `browser_driver_vm_policy()` was deleted and **both tiers now drive the production entry through `BrowserDriverManifest::resolve`** — the pin's equality assertion is `MICROVM_WORKER_BIN` itself, carried through `resolve()` → `build_launch_plan` → the hex `kastellan.worker=` token; the live tier uses the entry's own `wall_clock_ms` so a too-tight manifest value cannot be masked. **Verified by the negative case:** repointing the const at `target/debug/…` makes both the hermetic pin and the new resolve test FAIL loudly, then restored green. **DGX: full workspace `cargo test` 2590/0/48 + `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, 0 `[SKIP]` (+5 over the 2585 baseline = the 5 new tests); live VM tier GREEN on the production entry (CONNECT after 1.09 s).** Mac `cargo check -p kastellan-core --all-targets` clean. Spec/plan `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-07-19-browser-driver-microvm-entry*`. **Post-review `/fixall` (2026-07-19):** three review minors fixed on-branch — hermetic `wall_clock_ms`/`cpu_ms` pins (the live tier uses the entry's own wall-clock but is `#[ignore]`d, so the budgets had no always-on guard); shared `ResolveCtx::microvm_image_dir()` deduping the five per-manifest `KASTELLAN_MICROVM_DIR` default copies; `MICROVM_BROWSERS_PATH` ↔ Dockerfile-`ENV` coupling documented (`docker export` drops env metadata — the const is the sole runtime source). Re-verified: DGX full workspace **2590/0/48** + clippy clean, 0 `[SKIP]` (counts unchanged), live VM tier green (CONNECT 1.83 s).
  - **Slice 1 — the rootfs, 2026-07-19.** Unlike every sibling rootfs (assembled from scratch, libraries discovered by `ldd`), Chromium `dlopen`s NSS/fontconfig/SwiftShader which `ldd` cannot see, so `browser-driver.ext4` is staged by a **build-time-only** `Dockerfile.browser-driver` running `playwright install --with-deps` + `docker export`, rejoining the identical `mkfs.ext4 -d` tail — docker never appears at runtime. Installs only `chromium-headless-shell` (the worker launches `headless=True`; saves ~620 MB), `ROOTFS_MIB=1280` measured with a fail-closed fit check, and a build-time `headless_shell --dump-dom` smoke fails the image build on an incomplete dlopen closure. `/dev` questions settled by measurement (`CONFIG_DEVTMPFS_MOUNT=y`; `/dev/shm` absent but `--disable-dev-shm-usage` suffices, proven by a two-arm experiment) ⇒ **option D, no `microvm-init` change**, now guarded by a test pin rather than a comment. Tests: a hermetic launch-plan pin (in-rootfs guest path, no KVM needed) + a live DGX tier asserting the in-VM Chromium's `CONNECT` reaches a stub proxy — positive proof of launch, since a browser that fails to start emits none. **DGX 2585/0/48 + clippy clean.** **Slice 2 shipped this** (see the slice-2 bullet above; its §10.4 worry that `MICROVM_WORKER_BIN` matching the baked path was "nothing mechanically enforces that" is now enforced by the production-entry-driven pin). **Remaining: slice 3** (live render through a real egress sidecar; §10.4 also carries the untested-under-real-render `/dev/shm`-into-`/tmp` memory question).   - [x] **#459 residual #3 — entry-kind-aware `tool_allowlists` validation (CLOSES #459)** — the port-bearing-row blind spot turned out to be a symptom of a deeper gap: `tool_allowlists` conflated argv0-paths (`shell-exec`) and domains (`web-fetch`/`web-research`/`browser-driver`) under ONE `/`-prefix validator + SQL CHECK, so **domain rows were uninsertable through any path** (the CLI validator AND the `0009` CHECK both refused them; e2es only worked by passing domain vecs straight to the entry builders) — meaning operator content allowlists could not be configured at all, and the `localhost:8888` row the residual described could not even exist. Four layers: (1) `db::tool_allowlists` gains `EntryKind{Argv0,Domain}` + a pure hand-rolled `validate_domain` (no new dep; IPv6 via `std::net::Ipv6Addr`) accepting bare/wildcard domains, IPv4, and **bracketed IPv6 literals** while rejecting embedded ports/schemes/paths/userinfo/whitespace — closing the footgun at the source — dispatched by `validate_entry`; (2) **migration `0021`** gives the row its own **`kind` column** (`'argv0'|'domain'`, `DEFAULT 'argv0'`) with a `CASE`-on-kind CHECK (argv0 ⇒ absolute path; domain ⇒ bare/wildcard host or IPv4 or bracketed IPv6), so `localhost:8888` is refused at the SQL layer too **while preserving `0009`'s argv0 absolute-path guarantee** — a first kind-blind "union-branch" CHECK was caught by the full DGX gate as a real regression (a bare `echo` matches the domain branch, so shell-exec lost that guarantee; structurally unfixable without seeing the row's kind). Chosen over a `CASE WHEN tool IN (…)` CHECK because the tool roster grows: with the kind in the row, SQL never knows a tool name, so adding a tool needs **zero migrations** and only a new *kind* needs schema work; (3) `WorkerManifest::allowlist_kind()` + pure `registry_build::allowlist_kind_for_tool` classify each tool, threaded through `add`/`remove` → `cli_audit` → the CLI (unknown tools fall back to `Argv0`, back-compat; `InvalidDomain` is an exit-2 user error); (4) `endpoint_guard::host_is_malformed` folds a non-bracketed embedded colon **or a `/`** into the `NetScreen` dead set as defense-in-depth (`localhost:8888:443` and the legacy `/example.org:443` ⇒ Refuse/Warn; bracketed `[::1]:443` and wildcard `.example.org:443` never flagged) — screened in **every** routing mode, since a malformed host is undialable regardless, unlike a `localhost` name. **Net functional win: domain allowlists are operator-configurable for the first time.** **A `/review`→`/fixall` pass (2026-07-19) then closed two containment gaps that domain rows becoming insertable had newly exposed:** browser-driver fed rows into `Net::Allowlist` **verbatim**, and a bare-host entry is an **all-port** grant at the proxy (`allowed:host-only-entry`) — so an allowlisted `example.org` also reached `example.org:22`; now mapped through the same `allowlist_to_net_entries` (`:443`, HTTPS-only), with the worker's own check still getting the verbatim rows. And `allowlist_to_net_entries` **flattened wildcards** (`.example.org` → `example.org:443`), so the worker allowed `docs.example.org` while the proxy blocked it; the dot is now preserved (`.example.org:443`, which the proxy suffix-matches), making both enforcement points agree. Same pass: `remove` no longer shape-validates (validating it left pre-split `/example.org` rows **undeletable**), the all-dead `Refuse` detail names only the cause that fired, the audit payload records `kind`, and a `WORKER_MANIFESTS` invariant test pins `allowlist_tool()` ⇒ `allowlist_kind()`. Two live-PG gotchas recorded: `pg_get_constraintdef` renders `LIKE` as `~~` (definition-matching the old constraint on `'argv0 LIKE'` finds nothing), and `sqlx::migrate!` embeds at COMPILE time so a new `.sql` silently does not apply until `kastellan-db` is rebuilt. — **DGX full workspace 2584/0/47 + clippy `-D warnings` clean (new baseline; +8 from the entry-kind work, +4 more from the /fixall pass); Mac compile-check clean.** — branch `feat/459-tool-allowlists-entry-kind-validation`, **PR [#469](https://github.com/hherb/kastellan/pull/469) MERGED to `main` as `58d3e38e`** (issue #459 CLOSED), 2026-07-19. Spec/plan `docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-07-18-tool-allowlists-entry-kind-validation*`.
